<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the critical logarithmic correction for the nearest-neighbor cosine XY model on the square lattice. Let $`C_{b_c}(r)`$ be the two-point correlation at critical inverse temperature, obtained by taking the free-box thermodynamic limit while the two sites remain <i>r</i> lattice steps apart along a coordinate axis. Then
> 
> $`\displaystyle C_{b_c}(r)=B_{\mathrm{XY}}r^{-1/4}(\log r)^{1/8}(1+o(1)), \qquad B_{\mathrm{XY}}\in(0,\infty),`$
> 
> as $`r\to\infty`$.

- **claim-critical-logarithmic-equivalent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a constant $B_{\rm XY}\in(0,\infty)$ such that $$C_{b_c}(r) =B_{\rm XY}\,r^{-1/4}(\log r)^{1/8}(1+o(1)) \qquad(r\to\infty,\ r\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: The critical free-box thermodynamic correlation has a multiplicative logarithmic correction and a positive finite microscopic amplitude.
- **claim-spin-height-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-duality)): In a free spin box, the two-point correlation is $Z_G^\tau/Z_G^0$ on the dual graph with the exterior face fixed at height zero. One may represent that face by separate exterior incident copies, all pinned at zero, or identify those copies while retaining every edge. Circulations below are read in the dual with the exterior copies identified. The connection has circulations $+2\pi$ and $-2\pi$ around the two sources and can be supported on the dual bonds crossing a primal current path joining them.* *The center magnetization $M_n$ defined in <a href="#eq:center-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:center-definition">[eq:center-definition]</a> satisfies $M_n=\mathbb E_{n,b_c}^{\,0}e^{i\theta_0}>0$. It is $Z_G^\tau/Z_G^0$ on the free dual square with $2n$ dual vertices on each side, indexed by the primal cells, where $\tau$ has one circulation $2\pi$ around the center. It may be supported on the dual bonds crossing a primal current path from the center to the boundary.
  - Explanation: Spin correlations and fixed-boundary center magnetization equal positive Bessel-height sector ratios with their respective exterior-height conventions.
- **claim-finite-affine-comparisons** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-comparisons)): On every fixed primary graph, the Bessel height law has the following properties.* 1. *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same nonnegative quadratic precision is added to the affine and centered coordinates. Affine data may include an integral connection, observation centers, and compatible nonzero equality values. The monotonicity remains valid with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent, although the tilted ratio need not be at most one.* 2. *For zero affine data, centered covariances and centered real moment generating functions decrease under added centered precision. Centered characteristic coefficients allowed by the constant convention in both laws are nonnegative and increase under added precision. They are strictly positive for the finite-graph phases used below, whose coefficient total on every unconfined component is an integer.* 3. *If $X=\sum_e q_e\nabla_eh$ in a centered law, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}X\le C_b\sum_eq_e^2,\qquad \mathbb E\|X\|^k\le C_{k,b}\left(\sum_eq_e^2\right)^{k/2}.$$ More generally, after releasing endpoint, curl, and period constraints while retaining independent primary increments, a tilt with $\max_e\|q_e\|\le K$ costs at most $\exp(C_{b,K}\sum_eq_e^2)$.* *The statements include independent Gaussian observation noises. Released constants are taken modulo translations or confined; a real statistic used in releasing a constant must annihilate it.
  - Explanation: Added precision contracts affine ratios and centered real transforms, increases permitted centered characteristic coefficients, and gives primary-reference-cost moment bounds. The tilted affine ratio need not obey the un-tilted upper bound.
- **claim-critical-height-and-mixed-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-height-inputs)): For the critical parameter in <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>, the Bessel height coefficient of [[C](#ref-C)] is $$\label{eq:critical-coefficient} a=a(b_c)=8\pi.$$ At this parameter the following fixed-geometry statements hold.* 1. *Let the fixed geometry belong to the polygonal Lipschitz class of [[C](#ref-C), Theorem 3.1]: it is a finite union of bounded rectangular pieces with nondegenerate faces and widths. Pieces in one component are joined along full interfaces, which may be subdivided into segments; point contacts do not join components. All passages have positive width. Allow free walls and hard pins on the primary vertices in aligned unions of positive-area boxes. On each component $U$, fixed bounded, piecewise continuous density smears of the height have Gaussian Laplace and moment limits with covariance the inverse of $$a^{-1}\int_U\|\nabla v\|^2 \quad\text{on}\quad \{v\in H^1(U):v=0\text{ on the pin}\}.$$ For an unpinned component this is a statement about mean-free smears; its fractional mean modulo $2\pi$ becomes independent and uniform. Fixed finite-rank nonnegative penalties in box averages give the corresponding Gaussian laws, with constants integrated when the penalties confine them. The statements hold along every admissible mesh sequence, including bounded rounding of the fixed geometry. They include energy-approximable shifts in the sense of [[C](#ref-C), Theorem 3.1].* 2. *Start with no hard pins and use the centered law whose component-local box-average penalties confine every pre-pin component constant. Let $H,p$ be positively separated unions of boxes. Put $$D(H,p)=\log\frac{\mathbb P(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}{\mathbb P(h_H=0)},\qquad r_H(b^p)=\frac{\mathbb P(h_H=0\mid h_p=b^p)} {\mathbb P(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}.$$ Then $D(H,p)\ge0$ converges to the Gaussian pin interaction, $0<r_H\le1$, and $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{cen}} r_H=e^{-D(H,p)}$, where the expectation samples the guard data in the centered law. For every fixed finite list of these density smears and box averages, the conditional Laplace transforms and $r_H$ admit approximations by fixed finite lists of smooth observations of the guard data, whose laws and functions converge to their Gaussian counterparts. The list is enlarged after the lattice limit. These convergence assertions also hold when the data are sampled under a fixed finite real linear tilt; $D$ and $r_H$ retain their centered definitions above.
  - Explanation: The critical Bessel coefficient fixes the Gaussian energy normalization. Fixed mixed geometries admit height limits and sampled conditional guard approximations with the stated order of limits.
- **claim-center-and-bulk-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-comparison)): For integer sequences $n,n'\to\infty$ with $n'/n\to d\in(0,\infty)$, $$\label{geo:center-ratio} \frac{M_{n'}}{M_n}\longrightarrow d^{-1/8}.$$ There is a constant $c_{\rm shape}\in(0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{geo:bulk-ratio} \frac{C_{b_c}(r)}{M_r^2}\longrightarrow c_{\rm shape} \qquad(r\to\infty,\ r\in\mathbb Z).$$ Here $C_{b_c}$ is the thermodynamic limit in <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>; that limit precedes $r\to\infty$. In particular, $M_n=n^{-1/8+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: The same microscopic insertion costs cancel between square sizes and between bulk correlation and squared center magnetization. These comparisons give the rough center exponent and a positive finite shape factor.
- **claim-pinned-rim-trial-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-pinned-sector)): Let $U$ be a bounded connected polygonal Lipschitz domain in the fixed-geometry class of Proposition <a href="#prop:height-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:height-inputs">4</a>. Its outer boundary and the boundaries of its two holes are simple and rectilinear, with dyadic vertices and positive side lengths; the hole closures are disjoint and strictly inside the outer boundary, leaving positive-width passages. Let $\mathsf F$ be an aligned union of positive-area boxes with dyadic vertices in $U$, permitted as a pin in that fixed geometry. Suppose a simple dyadic rectilinear arc joins interiors of sides of the two holes transversely and has a neighborhood at positive distance from $\mathsf F$ and all nonincident boundaries. Give the holes periods $+2\pi,-2\pi$. Let $\vartheta=dv$ be a smooth closed one-form near $\overline U$ with these periods, with a multivalued primitive whose only jump is across that arc and whose branch is zero on a neighborhood of $\mathsf F$. Choose the canonical integral connections obtained from the jumps of this branch along a coherent rounded discretization of the arc, or connections related to them by integral gauges whose potentials vanish on every component of $\mathsf F$. Then, along every admissible rounded mesh sequence, $$\label{geo:pinned-sector-bound} \liminf_{\nu\to\infty} \Phi_{\mathcal G_\nu(U),\,h_{\mathsf F}=0}^\tau \ge \exp\left\{-\frac1{2a}\int_U\|\vartheta\|^2\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: A smooth trial connection with a branch vanishing near the retained rim supplies a lower bound for the pinned sector ratio. The admissible gauge must preserve the pin.
- **claim-exact-local-activity-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-local-map)): Assume every activity has period $\omega$ under common field translations, and its field dependence lies in the sites of its occupied support $X$ together with a fixed microscopic collar of width at most a fixed small fraction of $u$. The norm padding $D_u(X)$ is generally larger. Call an input small when its connected support has $\|X\|\le n_{\mathrm{sm}}$, for the fixed block threshold of the calculus. For a linear direction assume the point bound $$\|F\|_{u,X,\phi}\le \nu_X W_u^\kappa(X,\phi)e^{V_u(X,\phi)} \qquad\text{for every real }\phi,$$ with $\nu_X<\infty$; the resulting estimates are homogeneous in $\nu_X$.* *For a sufficiently large fixed dyadic $L$, sufficiently large weights $A_w$, and a sufficiently small activity ball, shell integration and designated localizations on small linear inputs give the exact identity $$\label{eq:exact-local-map} \mathbb E_\zeta\mathcal Z_u(K;\phi+\zeta) =\prod_{B\in\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_B)\, \mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\phi).$$ The map is analytic in the activities. If the total input size, including separately measured singleton coefficients, is $\nu$, its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,A_w}\nu^2$, with the corresponding differentiated bounds. A residual from a large input retains its whole coarse closure; only a piece from a small linear input can be assigned to a singleton. The coefficient $a_B$ is the complete sum of the field-independent pieces designated at $B$. Any undesignated constant value of a translated nonconstant piece remains in $K'$.* *Write $\Gamma_u=C_{<Lu}-C_{<u}$ for the shell from $u$ to $Lu$. For its estimates on the aligned block and observation grids of a finite square or torus of side $M$, assume $Lu\le M/D_0$, with $D_0$ a sufficiently large fixed range buffer. The norms <a href="#eq:point-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:point-norm">[eq:point-norm]</a>–<a href="#eq:activity-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:activity-norm">[eq:activity-norm]</a> have the shell composition, localization, charge, and large-support contractions of [[B](#ref-B), Sections 4 and 8, Lemma 9.2]. In particular, the extra regulator is monotone under enlargement, adds on compatible separated supports, and costs $C^{\|X\|}$ under shell averaging. On the full bulk block grid, a small neutral input invariant under field negation has gain $CL^{-3}$ after constant and quadratic localization. Removing only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for a neutral input invariant under field negation and $CL^{-1}$ for a general neutral input, including at irregular end blocks. For a charge-$k$ component of a small input, $k\ne0$, the Gaussian charge gain is $$\exp\left\{ -\bigl(\beta_{\mathrm{sh}}\|k\|\alpha-\tfrac12\beta_{\mathrm{sh}}^2\bigr) \Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)+h_0\|k\|\alpha\right\}.$$ Here a charge-$k$ component obeys $F(\phi+c)=e^{ik\alpha c}F(\phi)$, $v_0$ is a reference site in a block of $X$, and $\beta_{\mathrm{sh}}>0$ is the Cameron-shift amplitude. The analytic radius must satisfy $h_0>C_{\mathrm{sh}}\beta_{\mathrm{sh}}$, where $C_{\mathrm{sh}}$ is the fixed geometric constant for that shift, and the parameters are chosen so that $\beta_{\mathrm{sh}}\Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)>h_0$ by a uniform fixed margin. The displayed gains are then summable over $k\ne0$. This is [[B](#ref-B), Equation (4.26)]. Large inputs can be made an arbitrarily small linear contribution by increasing $A_w$.
  - Explanation: Shell integration preserves the partition functional through designated scalar extraction and coarse activities. Only small linear inputs may relocate pieces to singletons; large residuals keep their closures.
- **claim-centered-unit-background** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-center-background)): The branches can be chosen bounded, with a consistent additive constant, and for $0\le r\le4$ they satisfy, in lattice units, $$\label{ent:unit-decay} \begin{split} \|\nabla^r\eta_n(e)\| &\le C_r(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,0))^{-1-r},\\ \|\nabla^r(\eta_n-\eta_\infty)(e)\| &\le C_r n^{-1-r} \qquad\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,0)\le c_0n\bigr), \end{split}$$ where $c_0>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small. The first bound also holds for $\eta_\infty$. Differences at a free side use the reflected stencils of the local calculus. On a source-free rectangle with its required collar, there is a single-valued real lift $\lambda$ in reference-field units satisfying $$\nabla\lambda=\eta_n/\alpha .$$ Changing the integral gauge changes this lift by an integer multiple of the common period $\omega=2\pi/\alpha$.
  - Explanation: The minimizing circulation field has spatial difference bounds and agrees with the plane field to the stated central error. Source-free rectangles admit real lifts with a period ambiguity.
- **claim-reflected-entry-regulator** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-entry-regulator)): At these compatible block and observation sides, with strict reserve in the parameters of <a href="#eq:observation-regulator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:observation-regulator">[eq:observation-regulator]</a>, the shell composition, localization, and charge estimates in Proposition <a href="#prop:local-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:local-map">7</a> apply to $\Gamma_u$ in <a href="#ent:reference-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:reference-split">[ent:reference-split]</a> on an aligned free square of side $M$ when $Lu\le M/D_0$, with $D_0$ fixed and sufficiently large, using only internal faces in every open energy. The accumulated covariance $C_{<u}$ has the finite-range and open-observation contraction bounds used in $V_u$ and the localized entry determinant.
  - Explanation: The accumulated and shell covariance estimates extend to aligned free squares with internal-face open energies and the required range buffer.
- **claim-cutoff-suppression-and-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-cutoff-bounds)): There are $c>0$ and $s_1<\infty$, independent of the finite cell topology, such that for every $s\ge s_1$, every cut topology used below, and its centered sector or the restriction of the aligned unit sector, $$\label{ent:pointwise-cutoff} \frac{\widehat Z_G^\tau(y)}{Z_G^0(0)} \le \exp\left\{-c\sum_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2\right\} \qquad(y\hbox{ real}).$$* *For a connected full finite graph, let $\mathcal P_G^\tau(F)$ denote the integral of $F(y)Z_G^\tau(y)$ on the observation space modulo $y\mapsto y+2\pi k\mathbf1$, $k\in\mathbb Z$, and let $\widehat{\mathcal P}_G^\tau(F)$ use $\widehat Z_G^\tau$. For the centered sector take either a uniformly bounded function $F$ on this quotient, or $F=\ell(y)^k e^{t\ell(y)}$ times a uniformly bounded function on the quotient, where $k$ is a fixed nonnegative integer, $t$ is in a fixed compact subset of $\mathbb R$, and the mean-free linear observation $\ell$ has uniformly bounded primary reference cost and noise variance. For the square unit sector take $F=1$. Then $$\label{ent:cutoff-loss} \frac{\left\|\mathcal P_G^\tau(F) -\widehat{\mathcal P}_G^\tau(F)\right\|} {\mathcal P_G^0(1)} \le C_F\|\mathcal F(G)\|e^{-cs^2}.$$ In particular, if the logarithm of the side is $O(N)$ and $s\asymp N$, this bound is at most $e^{-c'N^2}$ for some $c'>0$. The same conclusion holds for the difference of normalized centered averages of the stated tests.
  - Explanation: The observation cutoff suppresses prescribed face differences while its physical loss is negligible for the listed tests. The unit-sector loss is absolute relative to the centered partition integral.
- **claim-face-energy-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-face-localization)): For the entry family just described, there is a number $D_s$, the same at every full junction, and a symmetric matrix $H_G$ such that $$\label{ent:face-decomposition} \mathcal Q_G(w)=D_s\sum_{z\in\mathcal V_4(G)}\sigma_z(w)^2 +\sum_{f,f'\in\mathcal F(G)}H_G(f,f')w_fw_{f'}, \qquad 0\le D_s\le C\log(2+s).$$ The second matrix need not be positive. In cell distances it has the bounds $$\label{ent:face-matrix-bounds} \begin{split} \|H_G(f,f')\|&\le Ce^{-c\,d(f,f')},\\ \|H_G(f,f')-H_{\widetilde G}(f,f')\| &\le C\min\left\{ e^{-c\,d(f,f')}, e^{-c\,d(\{f,f'\},\mathcal D)} \right\}. \end{split}$$ In the second line $\mathcal D$ contains the cells and faces where the two topologies or their local boundary rules differ, enlarged by the fixed stencil radius of the local trial, forcing, and constant quadratic coefficients used below. Extend absent entries by zero on the oriented face set of one common uncut inclusion $U$, and measure all distances there. The two decays can be combined after decreasing $c$.* *For the guarded comparison, let $E$ be the same still-retained local deletion in the current full graph and its current patch: $$G_1=G_0\setminus E,\qquad P_0=G_0\|_O,\qquad P_1=G_1\|_O=P_0\setminus E.$$ These four graphs belong to the stated tile and box family. The patch restrictions inherit every earlier cut and every retained observation row and local rule in $O$; their only new free wall is at the artificial boundary of $O$. On the common face set define $$\Delta=(H_{G_0}-H_{G_1})-(H_{P_0}-H_{P_1}).$$ Let $K_\Delta$ contain both before/after discrepancy sets and let $A_\Delta$ contain both full/patch discrepancy sets, including all absent exterior cells and faces and the artificial-boundary collar. Enlarge both by the same fixed stencil radius. If $d_U(K_\Delta,A_\Delta)\ge r$, then $$\label{ent:guarded-face-error} \|\Delta(f,f')\| \le C e^{-cr-c\,d_U(f,f')-c\,d_U(\{f,f'\},K_\Delta)}.$$ Shared physical free walls and earlier cuts are common data and do not belong to $A_\Delta$. Temporary hard primary guards are not among these four free-energy graphs. All constants are independent of $s$ and of the containing finite volume. For the physical centered or unit data this also gives $$\label{ent:face-energy-upper} E_G^\tau(y)\le C\sum_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2 +C\,1_{\{\text{unit junction is full in }G\}}\log(2+s).$$
  - Explanation: Face energy separates a common junction circulation cost from a symmetric matrix with exponential locality. A guarded deletion comparison retains decay in separation, pair distance, and distance from the deletion.
- **claim-cutoff-entry-compulsory-component** (construction, [source](#stmt-ent-initialization)): Fix the finite list of map norms and reserves just described. There are constants $C,c>0$ such that the following holds for every sufficiently large fixed dyadic $R$, with $P=R^5$ and the fixed period $q$. For every sufficiently large dyadic $s$ satisfying <a href="#ent:entry-side" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:entry-side">[ent:entry-side]</a>, the assertions below hold uniformly in the finite volume. The threshold on $s$ may depend on all preceding choices. A compatible torus or aligned free square has side at least $sP$; for a marked square its side is larger than the fixed multiple of $sP$ needed to contain the compulsory region and its collars. In the square applications its ratio to $sP$ tends to infinity.* 1. *There are plane activities $K_m^0$ which are real on real fields, negation-even, invariant under common translation by $\omega$, and covariant under block translations and square symmetries. There are centered torus and free-square activities $K_{\mathcal G,m}^0$ with $$\\|K_m^0\\|_{m,A_w,V}, \ \\|K_{\mathcal G,m}^0\\|_{m,A_w,V} \le Ce^{-cR}$$ in each of the fixed norms. On every such finite graph a positive field-independent scalar $c_{\mathcal G,m}$ makes <a href="#ent:ordinary-subset" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:ordinary-subset">[ent:ordinary-subset]</a> hold. If $D_m(X)$ unwraps in a torus, its activity is exactly the plane activity. The same is true in a free square when $D_m(X)$ misses the wall.* 2. *In a free square with the center unit there are real periodic activities $K_{\mathcal G,m}^\tau$ and $J_{\mathcal G,m}$ for which <a href="#ent:labelled-subset" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:labelled-subset">[ent:labelled-subset]</a> holds with that same scalar. For a constant $C_R<\infty$ independent of $s,n$, $$\label{ent:label-norm} \\|K_{\mathcal G,m}^\tau\\|_{m,A_w,V}\le Ce^{-cR}, \qquad \\|J_{\mathcal G,m}\\|_{m,A_w,V}\le C_Rs^{C_R}.$$ The sector norm may use the slightly weaker weight and stronger regulator already reserved above.* *There is a fixed set $\mathfrak C_{\star,m}$ of at most $C_R$ entry blocks, containing the full inflation of a square of side a fixed large multiple of $P$ in observation-cell units around the source, such that $$\label{ent:compulsory-support} J_{\mathcal G,m}(X)=0 \quad\hbox{unless}\quad \mathfrak C_{\star,m}\subset X.$$ Every block of $\mathfrak C_{\star,m}$ is occupied in $X$ in the subset functional. The full support of each summand of $J(X)$ also occupies every padded dependency and the complete connecting, winding, or wall-reaching tag of every item in that summand.* 3. *If the rectangle spanned by $D_m(X)$ misses the source, then $$\label{ent:translated-copy} K_{\mathcal G,m}^\tau(X,\psi) =K_{\mathcal G,m}^0(X,\psi+\lambda_X),$$ where $\lambda_X$ is its local lift. This assertion uses the finite-square centered prescription if the support is near a wall. There are also centered-unit plane prescriptions $K_{\infty,m}^\tau$ and $J_{\infty,m}$. Whenever $D_m(X)$ misses the square wall under the centered aligned identification just specified, its prescription is exactly the plane one with background translated by $$\label{ent:plane-background} (v_n-v_\infty)/\alpha .$$ This identity holds for both the unlabelled and labelled activities. The sufficient regions in all three copy statements include the padded supports and all occupied tags. The plane centered-unit prescriptions obey the same bounds as in <a href="#ent:label-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:label-norm">[ent:label-norm]</a>, with their local plane norms.
  - Explanation: Late compatible entries produce small ordinary activities and a compulsory labelled activity with polynomial norm growth. Exact copying requires the full padded support and every obstruction tag.
- **claim-analytic-pattern-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-pattern-estimate)): After the parameter choices in <a href="#ent:reserve-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:reserve-order">[ent:reserve-order]</a> and <a href="#ent:entry-side" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:entry-side">[ent:entry-side]</a>, and the threshold choice described below, there is $s_0<\infty$ such that for every admissible $s\ge s_0$, $$\label{ent:analytic-pattern-bound} \|F_\pi\|_{m,X,\psi} \le \mathcal C_\pi(s)\, C^{j_1+j_2}e^{-cR(j_1+j_2)} \prod_\ell Ce^{-c(R+d_\ell)} W_m^\kappa(X,\psi)e^{V_{j_0}(X,\psi)}$$ for all real backgrounds $\psi$. The point norm on the left is the full derivative sum in <a href="#eq:point-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:point-norm">[eq:point-norm]</a>. The constants are uniform in the compatible tori and free squares.
  - Explanation: Signed pattern factors retain exponential item and link gains in the full derivative norm, uniformly over real backgrounds and the compatible finite geometries.
- **claim-critical-bulk-trajectory** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-trajectory)): The entry choices can be made so that, for every fixed $0<c<c'<3$, uniformly for integers $c2^k\le j\le c'2^k$ as $k\to\infty$, $$\label{trj:unbalanced-conclusion} \begin{split} t_j^{(k)}&=\frac{1}{2(\log L)j} +O\!\left(\frac{\log(2+j)}{j^2}\right),\\ z_j^{(k)}&=O(j^{-1}),\qquad \\|\mathcal R_j^{(k)}\\|_{j,k}=O(j^{-2}). \end{split}$$ The constants may depend on $c,c'$ and the fixed map parameters. In particular the assertion applies at the stop $j'=N-O(\log N)$ in the history chosen for $2^k\le N<2^{k+1}$.
  - Explanation: The unbalanced gradient follows harmonic decay with a summable precision on fixed interior history windows. The fundamental singleton and remainder obey their stated decay orders.
- **claim-analytic-parity-interface** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-parity-interface)): There are a fixed small tube, $\vartheta<1$, and $C<\infty$, uniform over the entry sides above, on which the exact map satisfies $$\begin{aligned} u_+-u={}&-H_jZ^2+ O\bigl((\|u\|+Z^2)(Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)+\\|Q_e\\|^2 +\\|Q_o\\|(\|Z\|+\\|Q_o\\|)\bigr), \label{trj:u-map}\\ Z_+-Z={}&-b_juZ+ O\bigl((u^2+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)\|Z\| +( \|u\|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|+\\|Q_o\\|^2)\\|Q_o\\|\bigr), \label{trj:z-map}\\ \\|Q_{e,+}\\|\le{}&\vartheta\\|Q_e\\| +C(\|Z\|+\\|Q_o\\|)^2, \label{trj:even-map}\\ \\|Q_{o,+}\\|\le{}&\vartheta\\|Q_o\\| +C(\|u\|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)\|Z\|. \label{trj:odd-map} \end{aligned}$$ The error terms have the differentiated Taylor bounds of the displayed orders. The coefficients, independent of the entry side at a common absolute $j$, obey fixed positive upper and lower bounds for sufficiently large $j$. Their limits are $H>0$ and $$\label{trj:b-value} b_* =\frac{a}{4\pi}\log L=2\log L.$$
  - Explanation: Normal coordinates remove pure Gaussian drift. Half-period parity controls the leading coupled scalar terms and the even and odd contracting remainders.
- **claim-quadratic-coefficient-rate** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-coefficient-rate)): For the fixed nearest-neighbor reference and fixed $L$, there is $C<\infty$ such that, at every sufficiently large absolute index, $$\label{trj:coefficient-bound} \|H_j-H\|+\|b_j-b_*\|\le C(1+j)^{-2}.$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic map coefficients approach their limits at the stated absolute-index rate. This estimate concerns explicit Gaussian kernels and local algebra rather than a rate for height convergence.
- **claim-physical-torus-diagnostics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-trj-physical-torus)): Along the compatible even tori $n\to\infty$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_n^{\rm tor}e^{w x_n}&\longrightarrow e^{a\mathfrak g w^2/2},\label{trj:torus-laplace}\\ \mathbb E_n^{\rm tor}[e^{i\bar h}e^{w x_n}] -\mathbb E_n^{\rm tor}e^{i\bar h}\,e^{a\mathfrak g w^2/2} &\longrightarrow0.\label{trj:torus-factorization} \end{aligned}$$ The first assertion includes convergence of every fixed moment, and both assertions are locally uniform in complex $w$. They also hold for $X_{n,s},\chi_{n,s}$ when $s/n\to0$. For any prescribed small error in a fixed finite set of these diagnostics, one can first take $P$ sufficiently large and then $s$ sufficiently large so that the error is small uniformly over compatible $n\ge sP$.
  - Explanation: Mean-free torus diagnostics have Gaussian Laplace and moment limits, and the mean character factorizes from them. Observation versions require vanishing relative cell size.
- **claim-finite-history-confinement** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-vanishing)): For every sufficiently small prescribed tube, $R$ and the entry threshold can be chosen so that, for all sufficiently large $k$, every plane state through $j\le4\cdot2^k$ stays in that tube. For each $j\in\mathcal J_k$, the torus states at all stages $j_0(k)\le i\le j$ stay in the corresponding small ball. For every fixed $0<c<c'<4$, as $k\to\infty$, $$\label{trj:vanishing-ranges} \begin{split} \sup_{\substack{i\in\mathbb Z,\ j_0(k)\le i\\c2^k\le i\le c'2^k}} \bigl(\|t_i^{(k)}\|+\|z_i^{(k)}\| +\\|\mathcal R_i^{(k)}\\|_{i,k}\bigr)&\longrightarrow0,\\ \sup_{\substack{i\in\mathbb Z,\ j\in\mathcal J_k,\ j_0(k)\le i\le j\\ c2^k\le i\le c'2^k}} \bigl(\|t_i^{(k)}\|+\|z_i^{(k)}\| +\\|\mathcal R_{i\|j}^{\rm tor,(k)}\\|\bigr)&\longrightarrow0. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: Physical diagnostics rule out a first exit and force uniform vanishing of plane states and independently initialized torus remainders on interior history ranges.
- **claim-odd-cone-and-remainders** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-cone)): For every fixed $0<c<c'<3$, uniformly on $c2^k\le j\le c'2^k$ for large $k$, $$\label{trj:cone-bounds} \begin{gathered} Y_j=\|Z_j\|>0,\qquad Y_j\ge e^{-o(j)},\qquad Y_{j+1}/Y_j=1+o(1),\\ \\|Q_{e,j}\\|\le C Y_j^2,\qquad \\|Q_{o,j}\\|\le C(\|u_j\|+Y_j^2)Y_j. \end{gathered}$$ All little-oh statements are uniform on the indicated intervals.
  - Explanation: The physical phase lower bound prevents loss of the odd direction. Cone persistence yields slow scalar ratios and sharper even and odd remainder estimates.
- **claim-overlapping-history-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-trj-overlap)): Fix $0<c<c'<3$. Uniformly as $\min(k_1,k_2)\to\infty$, for every common integer $j$ satisfying $c2^{k_i}\le j\le c'2^{k_i}$ for $i=1,2$, put $(u_i,Y_i)=(u_j^{(k_i)},\|Z_j^{(k_i)}\|)$. Then $$\label{trj:overlap-bounds} \begin{split} \|u_1-u_2\|&\le C(Y_1^2+Y_2^2),\\ \|Y_1-Y_2\|&\le C\sum_{i=1}^2(\|u_i\|+Y_i)Y_i. \end{split}$$ The constants may depend on $c,c'$ and the fixed parameters. In particular $Y_1/Y_2\to1$ uniformly under these conditions.
  - Explanation: Histories at a common absolute stop are compared through the same finite physical torus. Cancelling that unknown diagnostic controls both normal scalars without a quantitative height-limit rate.
- **claim-consecutive-square-magnetization** (bound, [source](#stmt-obs-consecutive)): Fix $0<\epsilon<1/10$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $n_0=L^N$, $n_1=L^{N+1}$, and choose a power $m'=L^{j'}$ so that $$\label{obs:stop} D_N=\frac{n_0}{m'}\asymp N^\epsilon,\qquad j'=N-O(\log N).$$ Use the entry history for $N$ in both squares, and let $t_{j'}$ be its unbalanced plane gradient coefficient at the stop. Then $$\label{obs:consecutive-formula} \log\frac{M_{n_1}}{M_{n_0}} =-\frac{1-t_{j'}}{2a}(g_{n_1}-g_{n_0}) +O\left(\frac{D_N^4}{N^2}+\frac{1}{ND_N}\right).$$ Moreover, for a constant $c_G$, $$\label{obs:green-constant} g_{L^N}=2\pi(\log L)N+c_G+o(1).$$ All constants are independent of $N$.
  - Explanation: A common entry and stop express the consecutive center ratio through the stopped gradient coefficient and the circulation-energy difference with a summable error.
- **claim-paired-local-maps** (construction, [source](#stmt-obs-local-estimates)): The local maps can be chosen in both squares with the following properties. Here $\theta<1$ and $C$ depend only on the fixed parameters.* 1. *Untwisted activities on wall-free supports are the exact plane activities, and $$\label{obs:wall-recurrence} w_{j+1}\le \theta w_j+C U_j+C(U_j+w_j)^2.$$* 2. *On a shift-good support, $K_j^{\tau,n}$ is the corresponding untwisted activity evaluated at a local lift $\lambda_n$, with $\nabla\lambda_n=\eta_n/\alpha$. Near a wall this uses the finite-square untwisted activity. On defects, $$\label{obs:defect-recurrence} d_{j+1}\le\theta d_j+C(U_j+w_j)+C(U_j+w_j+d_j)^2.$$ On a central support whose complete causal neighborhood is wall-free in both squares, the unit activity is the common plane centered-unit prescription evaluated at $$\label{obs:smooth-background} \phi+\delta_n,\qquad \delta_n=\frac{v_n-v_\infty}{\alpha}.$$* 3. *The surviving label obeys $$\label{obs:label-recurrence} \\|J_{j+1}\\|_{j+1} \le \{\theta+C(U_j+w_j+d_j)\}\\|J_j\\|_j.$$ Every nonzero $J_j^n(X)$ has an occupied block within a fixed number of current blocks of the unit. Its support may extend farther. It has the same central plane-unit copy property as in <a href="#obs:smooth-background" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:smooth-background">[obs:smooth-background]</a>.* 4. *Choose $H_A$ in <a href="#obs:affected-set" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:affected-set">[obs:affected-set]</a> sufficiently large. For every output block outside $\mathcal A_j$, in either square, $$\label{obs:factor-outside} a_{jB}^{\tau,n}=a_{jB}^{0,n},\qquad \dot a_{jB}^{\tau,n}=0.$$ For every $B\in\mathcal A_j$, the complete sums satisfy $$\label{obs:factor-copy} a_{jB}^{0,n_0}=a_{jB}^{0,n_1},\qquad a_{jB}^{\tau,n_0}=a_{jB}^{\tau,n_1},\qquad \dot a_{jB}^{\tau,n_0}=\dot a_{jB}^{\tau,n_1}.$$ In the fixed small ball, choose $\rho<1$ with $\|a_{jB}^{\sigma,n}\|\le\rho$ for $\sigma\in\{0,\tau\}$. Then every output block satisfies $$\label{obs:scalar-log-bound} \left\|\log\frac{1+a_{jB}^{\tau,n}}{1+a_{jB}^{0,n}}\right\| \le C(U_j+w_j+d_j).$$
  - Explanation: Paired maps contract wall, defect, and labelled terms while making the complete extracted scalars agree between the square sizes near the insertion and cancel away from it.
- **claim-terminal-regulator-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-terminal-regulator)): With strict reserve in the parameters of <a href="#eq:observation-regulator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:observation-regulator">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> ($1<p_1<p_2$ and $0<h_*<1/p_2$), let $u$ be a later block side compatible with the fixed entry observation grid. Let the square or torus and its $u$-block grid be aligned and compatible, with side $M\ge2u$. Then, with the terminal covariance $C_{\ge u}$ from <a href="#ent:reference-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ent:reference-split">[ent:reference-split]</a>, for every compatible padded union $X$ of $u$-blocks and sufficiently small $\kappa$, $$\mathbb E\bigl[W_u^\kappa(X,\zeta)e^{V_u(X,\zeta)}\bigr] \le C^{\|X\|},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}(\zeta)=C_{\ge u}.$$ The terminal Gaussian is on nonconstant modes; the uniform common mean is integrated separately modulo $\omega$. This bound is uniform as $M/u$ increases.
  - Explanation: Terminal regulator moments remain controlled by occupied block cardinality as the terminal grid grows. The common constant is integrated separately.
- **claim-terminal-families-and-translations** (bound, [source](#stmt-obs-terminal-family)): Write $$W_u^\kappa(X,\phi)=\exp\{\kappa U_u^{\rm reg}(X,\phi)\}.$$ Here $U_u^{\rm reg}$ is the sum of gradient energy on $D_u(X)$, the $u$-weighted boundary trace, and the $u^4$-weighted block maxima of squared second differences. Its domain contains all boundary endpoints and fixed difference stencils, with even extension at a free wall. Let $$\mathfrak R_u^{\rm b}(X,\phi) =W_u^{\kappa_{\rm b}}(X,\phi)e^{V_{u,h_{\rm b}}(X,\phi)}$$ be the regulator used to measure terminal activities, where $V_{u,h}$ denotes <a href="#eq:observation-regulator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:observation-regulator">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> with $h_*=h$.* *There is a fixed $1<p_{\rm seg}<p_1$ with the following property. For a support $X$ of $k$ terminal blocks, put $D=D_{m'}(X)$. Suppose a real deterministic field $h_X$, allowed to depend on $n$ and $X$, is defined on the full domain just described and satisfies $$\label{obs:terminal-translation} U_{m'}^{\rm reg}(X,h_X)+\langle h_X,A_Dh_X\rangle\le C_{\rm tr}k$$ with one uniform constant. Then $$\label{obs:terminal-moment} \mathbb E\bigl[\mathfrak R_{m'}^{\rm b} (X,\zeta_{*,n}+h_X)^{p_{\rm seg}}\bigr]\le C^k.$$ The same bound holds at every point of a coupled segment between two such endpoints when their local regulator forms are identical. A random common anchor may be subtracted at each endpoint after neutral projection. The assertion for the segment is a bound on the moment at each parameter value, uniformly in that value.* *There are $H_N=O_L(D_N^2)$ terminal blocks in either square. Suppose every unlabelled terminal activity has norm at most $\nu$ in the weakest norm and put $b_N=CH_N\nu$. If $b_N=o(1)$, its terminal partition integral $T$ satisfies $$\label{obs:terminal-expansion} T=1+S_1+O(b_N^2),\qquad \|S_1\|\le b_N,\qquad \log T=S_1+O(b_N^2),$$ where $S_1$ is the sum of single-activity expectations. For a label direction of norm $v$, the derivative obeys $$\label{obs:terminal-derivative} \|\dot T\|\le CH_Nv e^{b_N}.$$ These estimates allow the constituent-dependent translations in <a href="#obs:terminal-translation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:terminal-translation">[obs:terminal-translation]</a>; their fixed per-block cost is absorbed by the weight.
  - Explanation: Full regulator and energy control of deterministic translations gives terminal moments and coupled-segment bounds. Small total activity permits a single-activity expansion and a bounded labelled derivative.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-height-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "For the critical parameter in <a href=\"#eq:critical-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:critical-definition\">[eq:critical-definition]</a>, the Bessel height coefficient of [[C](#ref-C)] is $$\\label{eq:critical-coefficient}\n                         a=a(b_c)=8\\pi.$$ At this parameter the following fixed-geometry statements hold.*\n\n1.  *Let the fixed geometry belong to the polygonal Lipschitz class of [[C](#ref-C), Theorem 3.1]: it is a finite union of bounded rectangular pieces with nondegenerate faces and widths. Pieces in one component are joined along full interfaces, which may be subdivided into segments; point contacts do not join components. All passages have positive width. Allow free walls and hard pins on the primary vertices in aligned unions of positive-area boxes. On each component $U$, fixed bounded, piecewise continuous density smears of the height have Gaussian Laplace and moment limits with covariance the inverse of $$a^{-1}\\int_U|\\nabla v|^2\n     \\quad\\text{on}\\quad\n     \\{v\\in H^1(U):v=0\\text{ on the pin}\\}.$$ For an unpinned component this is a statement about mean-free smears; its fractional mean modulo $2\\pi$ becomes independent and uniform. Fixed finite-rank nonnegative penalties in box averages give the corresponding Gaussian laws, with constants integrated when the penalties confine them. The statements hold along every admissible mesh sequence, including bounded rounding of the fixed geometry. They include energy-approximable shifts in the sense of [[C](#ref-C), Theorem 3.1].*\n\n2.  *Start with no hard pins and use the centered law whose component-local box-average penalties confine every pre-pin component constant. For every fixed finite list of these density smears and box averages, the conditional Laplace transforms and $r_H$ admit approximations by fixed finite lists of smooth observations of the guard data, whose laws and functions converge to their Gaussian counterparts. The list is enlarged after the lattice limit. These convergence assertions also hold when the data are sampled under a fixed finite real linear tilt; $D$ and $r_H$ retain their centered definitions above.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-pinned-sector",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a bounded connected polygonal Lipschitz domain in the fixed-geometry class of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:height-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:height-inputs\">4</a>. Its outer boundary and the boundaries of its two holes are simple and rectilinear, with dyadic vertices and positive side lengths; the hole closures are disjoint and strictly inside the outer boundary, leaving positive-width passages. Let $\\mathsf F$ be an aligned union of positive-area boxes with dyadic vertices in $U$, permitted as a pin in that fixed geometry. Suppose a simple dyadic rectilinear arc joins interiors of sides of the two holes transversely and has a neighborhood at positive distance from $\\mathsf F$ and all nonincident boundaries. Give the holes periods $+2\\pi,-2\\pi$. Let $\\vartheta=dv$ be a smooth closed one-form near $\\overline U$ with these periods, with a multivalued primitive whose only jump is across that arc and whose branch is zero on a neighborhood of $\\mathsf F$. Choose the canonical integral connections obtained from the jumps of this branch along a coherent rounded discretization of the arc, or connections related to them by integral gauges whose potentials vanish on every component of $\\mathsf F$. Then, along every admissible rounded mesh sequence, $$\\label{geo:pinned-sector-bound}\n \\liminf_{\\nu\\to\\infty}\n       \\Phi_{\\mathcal G_\\nu(U),\\,h_{\\mathsf F}=0}^\\tau\n       \\ge \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1{2a}\\int_U|\\vartheta|^2\\right\\}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-pinned-sector",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a bounded connected polygonal Lipschitz domain in the fixed-geometry class of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:height-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:height-inputs\">4</a>. Its outer boundary and the boundaries of its two holes are simple and rectilinear, with dyadic vertices and positive side lengths; the hole closures are disjoint and strictly inside the outer boundary, leaving positive-width passages. Let $\\mathsf F$ be an aligned union of positive-area boxes with dyadic vertices in $U$, permitted as a pin in that fixed geometry. Suppose a simple dyadic rectilinear arc joins interiors of sides of the two holes transversely and has a neighborhood at positive distance from $\\mathsf F$ and all nonincident boundaries. Give the holes periods $+2\\pi,-2\\pi$. Let $\\vartheta=dv$ be a smooth closed one-form near $\\overline U$ with these periods, with a multivalued primitive whose only jump is across that arc and whose branch is zero on a neighborhood of $\\mathsf F$. Choose the canonical integral connections obtained from the jumps of this branch along a coherent rounded discretization of the arc, or connections related to them by integral gauges whose potentials vanish on every component of $\\mathsf F$. Then, along every admissible rounded mesh sequence, $$\\label{geo:pinned-sector-bound}\n \\liminf_{\\nu\\to\\infty}\n       \\Phi_{\\mathcal G_\\nu(U),\\,h_{\\mathsf F}=0}^\\tau\n       \\ge \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1{2a}\\int_U|\\vartheta|^2\\right\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $U$ be a bounded connected polygonal Lipschitz domain in the fixed-geometry class of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:height-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:height-inputs\">4</a>. Its outer boundary and the boundaries of its two holes are simple and rectilinear, with dyadic vertices and positive side lengths; the hole closures are disjoint and strictly inside the outer boundary, leaving positive-width passages. Let $\\mathsf F$ be an aligned union of positive-area boxes with dyadic vertices in $U$, permitted as a pin in that fixed geometry. Suppose a simple dyadic rectilinear arc joins interiors of sides of the two holes transversely and has a neighborhood at positive distance from $\\mathsf F$ and all nonincident boundaries. Give the holes periods $+2\\pi,-2\\pi$. Let $\\vartheta=dv$ be a smooth closed one-form near $\\overline U$ with these periods, with a multivalued primitive whose only jump is across that arc and whose branch is zero on a neighborhood of $\\mathsf F$. Choose the canonical integral connections obtained from the jumps of this branch along a coherent rounded discretization of the arc, or connections related to them by integral gauges whose potentials vanish on every component of $\\mathsf F$. Then, along every admissible rounded mesh sequence, $$\\label{geo:pinned-sector-bound}\n \\liminf_{\\nu\\to\\infty}\n       \\Phi_{\\mathcal G_\\nu(U),\\,h_{\\mathsf F}=0}^\\tau\n       \\ge \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1{2a}\\int_U|\\vartheta|^2\\right\\}.$$",
      "explanation": "A smooth trial connection with a branch vanishing near the retained rim supplies a lower bound for the pinned sector ratio. The admissible gauge must preserve the pin.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-local-map",
        "exact_quote": "Assume every activity has period $\\omega$ under common field translations, and its field dependence lies in the sites of its occupied support $X$ together with a fixed microscopic collar of width at most a fixed small fraction of $u$. The norm padding $D_u(X)$ is generally larger. Call an input small when its connected support has $|X|\\le n_{\\mathrm{sm}}$, for the fixed block threshold of the calculus. For a linear direction assume the point bound $$|F|_{u,X,\\phi}\\le\n \\nu_X W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)e^{V_u(X,\\phi)}\n \\qquad\\text{for every real }\\phi,$$ with $\\nu_X<\\infty$; the resulting estimates are homogeneous in $\\nu_X$.*\n\n*For a sufficiently large fixed dyadic $L$, sufficiently large weights $A_w$, and a sufficiently small activity ball, shell integration and designated localizations on small linear inputs give the exact identity $$\\label{eq:exact-local-map}\n \\mathbb E_\\zeta\\mathcal Z_u(K;\\phi+\\zeta)\n =\\prod_{B\\in\\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_B)\\,\n        \\mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\\phi).$$ The map is analytic in the activities. If the total input size, including separately measured singleton coefficients, is $\\nu$, its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,A_w}\\nu^2$, with the corresponding differentiated bounds. A residual from a large input retains its whole coarse closure; only a piece from a small linear input can be assigned to a singleton. The coefficient $a_B$ is the complete sum of the field-independent pieces designated at $B$. Any undesignated constant value of a translated nonconstant piece remains in $K'$.*\n\n*Write $\\Gamma_u=C_{<Lu}-C_{<u}$ for the shell from $u$ to $Lu$. For its estimates on the aligned block and observation grids of a finite square or torus of side $M$, assume $Lu\\le M/D_0$, with $D_0$ a sufficiently large fixed range buffer. The norms <a href=\"#eq:point-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:point-norm\">[eq:point-norm]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:activity-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:activity-norm\">[eq:activity-norm]</a> have the shell composition, localization, charge, and large-support contractions of [[B](#ref-B), Sections 4 and 8, Lemma 9.2]. In particular, the extra regulator is monotone under enlargement, adds on compatible separated supports, and costs $C^{|X|}$ under shell averaging. On the full bulk block grid, a small neutral input invariant under field negation has gain $CL^{-3}$ after constant and quadratic localization. Removing only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for a neutral input invariant under field negation and $CL^{-1}$ for a general neutral input, including at irregular end blocks. For a charge-$k$ component of a small input, $k\\ne0$, the Gaussian charge gain is $$\\exp\\left\\{\n -\\bigl(\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}|k|\\alpha-\\tfrac12\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}^2\\bigr)\n       \\Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)+h_0|k|\\alpha\\right\\}.$$ Here a charge-$k$ component obeys $F(\\phi+c)=e^{ik\\alpha c}F(\\phi)$, $v_0$ is a reference site in a block of $X$, and $\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}>0$ is the Cameron-shift amplitude. The analytic radius must satisfy $h_0>C_{\\mathrm{sh}}\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}$, where $C_{\\mathrm{sh}}$ is the fixed geometric constant for that shift, and the parameters are chosen so that $\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}\\Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)>h_0$ by a uniform fixed margin. The displayed gains are then summable over $k\\ne0$. This is [[B](#ref-B), Equation (4.26)]. Large inputs can be made an arbitrarily small linear contribution by increasing $A_w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-center-background",
        "exact_quote": "The branches can be chosen bounded, with a consistent additive constant, and for $0\\le r\\le4$ they satisfy, in lattice units, $$\\label{ent:unit-decay}\n\\begin{split}\n |\\nabla^r\\eta_n(e)|\n   &\\le C_r(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,0))^{-1-r},\\\\\n |\\nabla^r(\\eta_n-\\eta_\\infty)(e)|\n   &\\le C_r n^{-1-r}\n       \\qquad\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,0)\\le c_0n\\bigr),\n\\end{split}$$ where $c_0>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small. The first bound also holds for $\\eta_\\infty$. Differences at a free side use the reflected stencils of the local calculus. On a source-free rectangle with its required collar, there is a single-valued real lift $\\lambda$ in reference-field units satisfying $$\\nabla\\lambda=\\eta_n/\\alpha .$$ Changing the integral gauge changes this lift by an integer multiple of the common period $\\omega=2\\pi/\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-center-background",
          "exact_quote": "The branches can be chosen bounded, with a consistent additive constant, and for $0\\le r\\le4$ they satisfy, in lattice units, $$\\label{ent:unit-decay}\n\\begin{split}\n |\\nabla^r\\eta_n(e)|\n   &\\le C_r(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,0))^{-1-r},\\\\\n |\\nabla^r(\\eta_n-\\eta_\\infty)(e)|\n   &\\le C_r n^{-1-r}\n       \\qquad\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,0)\\le c_0n\\bigr),\n\\end{split}$$ where $c_0>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small. The first bound also holds for $\\eta_\\infty$. Differences at a free side use the reflected stencils of the local calculus. On a source-free rectangle with its required collar, there is a single-valued real lift $\\lambda$ in reference-field units satisfying $$\\nabla\\lambda=\\eta_n/\\alpha .$$ Changing the integral gauge changes this lift by an integer multiple of the common period $\\omega=2\\pi/\\alpha$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-ent-entry-regulator",
        "exact_quote": "At these compatible block and observation sides, with strict reserve in the parameters of <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a>, the shell composition, localization, and charge estimates in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-map\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-map\">7</a> apply to $\\Gamma_u$ in <a href=\"#ent:reference-split\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:reference-split\">[ent:reference-split]</a> on an aligned free square of side $M$ when $Lu\\le M/D_0$, with $D_0$ fixed and sufficiently large, using only internal faces in every open energy.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-cutoff-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "There are $c>0$ and $s_1<\\infty$, independent of the finite cell topology, such that for every $s\\ge s_1$, every cut topology used below, and its centered sector or the restriction of the aligned unit sector, $$\\label{ent:pointwise-cutoff}\n \\frac{\\widehat Z_G^\\tau(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le \\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2\\right\\}\n                    \\qquad(y\\hbox{ real}).$$*\n\n\n*For a connected full finite graph, let $\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)$ denote the integral of $F(y)Z_G^\\tau(y)$ on the observation space modulo $y\\mapsto y+2\\pi k\\mathbf1$, $k\\in\\mathbb Z$, and let $\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)$ use $\\widehat Z_G^\\tau$. For the centered sector take either a uniformly bounded function $F$ on this quotient, or $F=\\ell(y)^k e^{t\\ell(y)}$ times a uniformly bounded function on the quotient, where $k$ is a fixed nonnegative integer, $t$ is in a fixed compact subset of $\\mathbb R$, and the mean-free linear observation $\\ell$ has uniformly bounded primary reference cost and noise variance. For the square unit sector take $F=1$. Then $$\\label{ent:cutoff-loss}\n \\frac{\\left|\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)\n             -\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)\\right|}\n      {\\mathcal P_G^0(1)}\n       \\le C_F|\\mathcal F(G)|e^{-cs^2}.$$ In particular, if the logarithm of the side is $O(N)$ and $s\\asymp N$, this bound is at most $e^{-c'N^2}$ for some $c'>0$. The same conclusion holds for the difference of normalized centered averages of the stated tests.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-cutoff-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "There are $c>0$ and $s_1<\\infty$, independent of the finite cell topology, such that for every $s\\ge s_1$, every cut topology used below, and its centered sector or the restriction of the aligned unit sector, $$\\label{ent:pointwise-cutoff}\n \\frac{\\widehat Z_G^\\tau(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le \\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2\\right\\}\n                    \\qquad(y\\hbox{ real}).$$*\n\n\n*For a connected full finite graph, let $\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)$ denote the integral of $F(y)Z_G^\\tau(y)$ on the observation space modulo $y\\mapsto y+2\\pi k\\mathbf1$, $k\\in\\mathbb Z$, and let $\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)$ use $\\widehat Z_G^\\tau$. For the centered sector take either a uniformly bounded function $F$ on this quotient, or $F=\\ell(y)^k e^{t\\ell(y)}$ times a uniformly bounded function on the quotient, where $k$ is a fixed nonnegative integer, $t$ is in a fixed compact subset of $\\mathbb R$, and the mean-free linear observation $\\ell$ has uniformly bounded primary reference cost and noise variance. For the square unit sector take $F=1$. Then $$\\label{ent:cutoff-loss}\n \\frac{\\left|\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)\n             -\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)\\right|}\n      {\\mathcal P_G^0(1)}\n       \\le C_F|\\mathcal F(G)|e^{-cs^2}.$$ In particular, if the logarithm of the side is $O(N)$ and $s\\asymp N$, this bound is at most $e^{-c'N^2}$ for some $c'>0$. The same conclusion holds for the difference of normalized centered averages of the stated tests.",

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are $c>0$ and $s_1<\\infty$, independent of the finite cell topology, such that for every $s\\ge s_1$, every cut topology used below, and its centered sector or the restriction of the aligned unit sector, $$\\label{ent:pointwise-cutoff}\n \\frac{\\widehat Z_G^\\tau(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le \\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2\\right\\}\n                    \\qquad(y\\hbox{ real}).$$*\n\n\n*For a connected full finite graph, let $\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)$ denote the integral of $F(y)Z_G^\\tau(y)$ on the observation space modulo $y\\mapsto y+2\\pi k\\mathbf1$, $k\\in\\mathbb Z$, and let $\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)$ use $\\widehat Z_G^\\tau$. For the centered sector take either a uniformly bounded function $F$ on this quotient, or $F=\\ell(y)^k e^{t\\ell(y)}$ times a uniformly bounded function on the quotient, where $k$ is a fixed nonnegative integer, $t$ is in a fixed compact subset of $\\mathbb R$, and the mean-free linear observation $\\ell$ has uniformly bounded primary reference cost and noise variance. For the square unit sector take $F=1$. Then $$\\label{ent:cutoff-loss}\n \\frac{\\left|\\mathcal P_G^\\tau(F)\n             -\\widehat{\\mathcal P}_G^\\tau(F)\\right|}\n      {\\mathcal P_G^0(1)}\n       \\le C_F|\\mathcal F(G)|e^{-cs^2}.$$ In particular, if the logarithm of the side is $O(N)$ and $s\\asymp N$, this bound is at most $e^{-c'N^2}$ for some $c'>0$. The same conclusion holds for the difference of normalized centered averages of the stated tests.",
      "explanation": "The observation cutoff suppresses prescribed face differences while its physical loss is negligible for the listed tests. The unit-sector loss is absolute relative to the centered partition integral.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-face-localization",
        "exact_quote": "For the entry family just described, there is a number $D_s$, the same at every full junction, and a symmetric matrix $H_G$ such that $$\\label{ent:face-decomposition}\n \\mathcal Q_G(w)=D_s\\sum_{z\\in\\mathcal V_4(G)}\\sigma_z(w)^2\n             +\\sum_{f,f'\\in\\mathcal F(G)}H_G(f,f')w_fw_{f'},\n \\qquad 0\\le D_s\\le C\\log(2+s).$$ The second matrix need not be positive. In cell distances it has the bounds $$\\label{ent:face-matrix-bounds}\n\\begin{split}\n |H_G(f,f')|&\\le Ce^{-c\\,d(f,f')},\\\\\n |H_G(f,f')-H_{\\widetilde G}(f,f')|\n &\\le C\\min\\left\\{\n       e^{-c\\,d(f,f')},\n       e^{-c\\,d(\\{f,f'\\},\\mathcal D)}\n                    \\right\\}.\n\\end{split}$$ In the second line $\\mathcal D$ contains the cells and faces where the two topologies or their local boundary rules differ, enlarged by the fixed stencil radius of the local trial, forcing, and constant quadratic coefficients used below. Extend absent entries by zero on the oriented face set of one common uncut inclusion $U$, and measure all distances there. The two decays can be combined after decreasing $c$.*\n\n\n\n*For the guarded comparison, let $E$ be the same still-retained local deletion in the current full graph and its current patch: $$G_1=G_0\\setminus E,\\qquad\n P_0=G_0|_O,\\qquad P_1=G_1|_O=P_0\\setminus E.$$ These four graphs belong to the stated tile and box family. The patch restrictions inherit every earlier cut and every retained observation row and local rule in $O$; their only new free wall is at the artificial boundary of $O$. On the common face set define $$\\Delta=(H_{G_0}-H_{G_1})-(H_{P_0}-H_{P_1}).$$ Let $K_\\Delta$ contain both before/after discrepancy sets and let $A_\\Delta$ contain both full/patch discrepancy sets, including all absent exterior cells and faces and the artificial-boundary collar. Enlarge both by the same fixed stencil radius. If $d_U(K_\\Delta,A_\\Delta)\\ge r$, then $$\\label{ent:guarded-face-error}\n |\\Delta(f,f')|\n \\le C e^{-cr-c\\,d_U(f,f')-c\\,d_U(\\{f,f'\\},K_\\Delta)}.$$ Shared physical free walls and earlier cuts are common data and do not belong to $A_\\Delta$. Temporary hard primary guards are not among these four free-energy graphs. All constants are independent of $s$ and of the containing finite volume. For the physical centered or unit data this also gives $$\\label{ent:face-energy-upper}\n E_G^\\tau(y)\\le C\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}w_f^2\n       +C\\,1_{\\{\\text{unit junction is full in }G\\}}\\log(2+s).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-initialization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the finite list of map norms and reserves just described. There are constants $C,c>0$ such that the following holds for every sufficiently large fixed dyadic $R$, with $P=R^5$ and the fixed period $q$. For every sufficiently large dyadic $s$ satisfying <a href=\"#ent:entry-side\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:entry-side\">[ent:entry-side]</a>, the assertions below hold uniformly in the finite volume. The threshold on $s$ may depend on all preceding choices. A compatible torus or aligned free square has side at least $sP$; for a marked square its side is larger than the fixed multiple of $sP$ needed to contain the compulsory region and its collars. In the square applications its ratio to $sP$ tends to infinity.*\n\n1.  *There are plane activities $K_m^0$ which are real on real fields, negation-even, invariant under common translation by $\\omega$, and covariant under block translations and square symmetries. There are centered torus and free-square activities $K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0$ with $$\\|K_m^0\\|_{m,A_w,V},\n                      \\ \\|K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0\\|_{m,A_w,V}\n                             \\le Ce^{-cR}$$ in each of the fixed norms. On every such finite graph a positive field-independent scalar $c_{\\mathcal G,m}$ makes <a href=\"#ent:ordinary-subset\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:ordinary-subset\">[ent:ordinary-subset]</a> hold. If $D_m(X)$ unwraps in a torus, its activity is exactly the plane activity. The same is true in a free square when $D_m(X)$ misses the wall.*\n\n\n2.  *In a free square with the center unit there are real periodic activities $K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau$ and $J_{\\mathcal G,m}$ for which <a href=\"#ent:labelled-subset\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:labelled-subset\">[ent:labelled-subset]</a> holds with that same scalar. For a constant $C_R<\\infty$ independent of $s,n$, $$\\label{ent:label-norm}\n     \\|K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau\\|_{m,A_w,V}\\le Ce^{-cR},\n     \\qquad\n     \\|J_{\\mathcal G,m}\\|_{m,A_w,V}\\le C_Rs^{C_R}.$$ The sector norm may use the slightly weaker weight and stronger regulator already reserved above.*\n\n    *There is a fixed set $\\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}$ of at most $C_R$ entry blocks, containing the full inflation of a square of side a fixed large multiple of $P$ in observation-cell units around the source, such that $$\\label{ent:compulsory-support}\n           J_{\\mathcal G,m}(X)=0\n                      \\quad\\hbox{unless}\\quad\n                      \\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}\\subset X.$$ Every block of $\\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}$ is occupied in $X$ in the subset functional. The full support of each summand of $J(X)$ also occupies every padded dependency and the complete connecting, winding, or wall-reaching tag of every item in that summand.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If the rectangle spanned by $D_m(X)$ misses the source, then $$\\label{ent:translated-copy}\n     K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau(X,\\psi)\n                 =K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0(X,\\psi+\\lambda_X),$$ where $\\lambda_X$ is its local lift. This assertion uses the finite-square centered prescription if the support is near a wall. There are also centered-unit plane prescriptions $K_{\\infty,m}^\\tau$ and $J_{\\infty,m}$. Whenever $D_m(X)$ misses the square wall under the centered aligned identification just specified, its prescription is exactly the plane one with background translated by $$\\label{ent:plane-background}\n                             (v_n-v_\\infty)/\\alpha .$$ This identity holds for both the unlabelled and labelled activities. The sufficient regions in all three copy statements include the padded supports and all occupied tags. The plane centered-unit prescriptions obey the same bounds as in <a href=\"#ent:label-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:label-norm\">[ent:label-norm]</a>, with their local plane norms.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-pattern-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "After the parameter choices in <a href=\"#ent:reserve-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:reserve-order\">[ent:reserve-order]</a> and <a href=\"#ent:entry-side\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:entry-side\">[ent:entry-side]</a>, and the threshold choice described below, there is $s_0<\\infty$ such that for every admissible $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{ent:analytic-pattern-bound}\n |F_\\pi|_{m,X,\\psi}\n \\le \\mathcal C_\\pi(s)\\,\n       C^{j_1+j_2}e^{-cR(j_1+j_2)}\n       \\prod_\\ell Ce^{-c(R+d_\\ell)}\n       W_m^\\kappa(X,\\psi)e^{V_{j_0}(X,\\psi)}$$ for all real backgrounds $\\psi$. The point norm on the left is the full derivative sum in <a href=\"#eq:point-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:point-norm\">[eq:point-norm]</a>. The constants are uniform in the compatible tori and free squares.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-pattern-estimate",
          "exact_quote": "After the parameter choices in <a href=\"#ent:reserve-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:reserve-order\">[ent:reserve-order]</a> and <a href=\"#ent:entry-side\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:entry-side\">[ent:entry-side]</a>, and the threshold choice described below, there is $s_0<\\infty$ such that for every admissible $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{ent:analytic-pattern-bound}\n |F_\\pi|_{m,X,\\psi}\n \\le \\mathcal C_\\pi(s)\\,\n       C^{j_1+j_2}e^{-cR(j_1+j_2)}\n       \\prod_\\ell Ce^{-c(R+d_\\ell)}\n       W_m^\\kappa(X,\\psi)e^{V_{j_0}(X,\\psi)}$$ for all real backgrounds $\\psi$.         "anchor": "stmt-trj-parity-interface",
        "exact_quote": "There are a fixed small tube, $\\vartheta<1$, and $C<\\infty$, uniform over the entry sides above, on which the exact map satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n u_+-u={}&-H_jZ^2+\n O\\bigl((|u|+Z^2)(Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)+\\|Q_e\\|^2\n                    +\\|Q_o\\|(|Z|+\\|Q_o\\|)\\bigr),\n                                      \\label{trj:u-map}\\\\\n Z_+-Z={}&-b_juZ+\n O\\bigl((u^2+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)|Z|\n       +( |u|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|+\\|Q_o\\|^2)\\|Q_o\\|\\bigr),\n                                      \\label{trj:z-map}\\\\\n \\|Q_{e,+}\\|\\le{}&\\vartheta\\|Q_e\\|\n                         +C(|Z|+\\|Q_o\\|)^2,\n                                      \\label{trj:even-map}\\\\\n \\|Q_{o,+}\\|\\le{}&\\vartheta\\|Q_o\\|\n                         +C(|u|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)|Z|.\n                                      \\label{trj:odd-map}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The error terms have the differentiated Taylor bounds of the displayed orders. The coefficients, independent of the entry side at a common absolute $j$, obey fixed positive upper and lower bounds for sufficiently large $j$. Their limits are $H>0$ and $$\\label{trj:b-value}\n                       b_* =\\frac{a}{4\\pi}\\log L=2\\log L.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trj-physical-torus",
        "exact_quote": "Along the compatible even tori $n\\to\\infty$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_n^{\\rm tor}e^{w x_n}&\\longrightarrow\n                  e^{a\\mathfrak g w^2/2},\\label{trj:torus-laplace}\\\\\n \\mathbb E_n^{\\rm tor}[e^{i\\bar h}e^{w x_n}]\n   -\\mathbb E_n^{\\rm tor}e^{i\\bar h}\\,e^{a\\mathfrak g w^2/2}\n       &\\longrightarrow0.\\label{trj:torus-factorization}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first assertion includes convergence of every fixed moment, and both assertions are locally uniform in complex $w$. They also hold for $X_{n,s},\\chi_{n,s}$ when $s/n\\to0$. For any prescribed small error in a fixed finite set of these diagnostics, one can first take $P$ sufficiently large and then $s$ sufficiently large so that the error is small uniformly over compatible $n\\ge sP$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },

They also hold for $X_{n,s},\\chi_{n,s}$ when $s/n\\to0$. For any prescribed small error in a fixed finite set of these diagnostics, one can first take $P$ sufficiently large and then $s$ sufficiently large so that the error is small uniformly over compatible $n\\ge sP$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trj-cone",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $0<c<c'<3$, uniformly on $c2^k\\le j\\le c'2^k$ for large $k$, $$\\label{trj:cone-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n Y_j=|Z_j|>0,\\qquad Y_j\\ge e^{-o(j)},\\qquad\n                       Y_{j+1}/Y_j=1+o(1),\\\\\n \\|Q_{e,j}\\|\\le C Y_j^2,\\qquad\n \\|Q_{o,j}\\|\\le C(|u_j|+Y_j^2)Y_j.\n \\end{gathered}$$ All little-oh statements are uniform on the indicated intervals.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c<c'<3$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-consecutive",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\epsilon<1/10$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $n_0=L^N$, $n_1=L^{N+1}$, and choose a power $m'=L^{j'}$ so that $$\\label{obs:stop}\n D_N=\\frac{n_0}{m'}\\asymp N^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n j'=N-O(\\log N).$$ Use the entry history for $N$ in both squares, and let $t_{j'}$ be its unbalanced plane gradient coefficient at the stop. Then $$\\label{obs:consecutive-formula}\n \\log\\frac{M_{n_1}}{M_{n_0}}\n =-\\frac{1-t_{j'}}{2a}(g_{n_1}-g_{n_0})\n   +O\\left(\\frac{D_N^4}{N^2}+\\frac{1}{ND_N}\\right).$$ Moreover, for a constant $c_G$, $$\\label{obs:green-constant}\n                g_{L^N}=2\\pi(\\log L)N+c_G+o(1).$$ All constants are independent of $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-consecutive",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\epsilon<1/10$.           "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-local-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "The local maps can be chosen in both squares with the following properties. Here $\\theta<1$ and $C$ depend only on the fixed parameters.*\n\n\n1.  *Untwisted activities on wall-free supports are the exact plane activities, and $$\\label{obs:wall-recurrence}\n     w_{j+1}\\le \\theta w_j+C U_j+C(U_j+w_j)^2.$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *On a shift-good support, $K_j^{\\tau,n}$ is the corresponding untwisted activity evaluated at a local lift $\\lambda_n$, with $\\nabla\\lambda_n=\\eta_n/\\alpha$. Near a wall this uses the finite-square untwisted activity. On defects, $$\\label{obs:defect-recurrence}\n     d_{j+1}\\le\\theta d_j+C(U_j+w_j)+C(U_j+w_j+d_j)^2.$$ On a central support whose complete causal neighborhood is wall-free in both squares, the unit activity is the common plane centered-unit prescription evaluated at $$\\label{obs:smooth-background}\n                         \\phi+\\delta_n,\\qquad\n              \\delta_n=\\frac{v_n-v_\\infty}{\\alpha}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *The surviving label obeys $$\\label{obs:label-recurrence}\n     \\|J_{j+1}\\|_{j+1}\n     \\le \\{\\theta+C(U_j+w_j+d_j)\\}\\|J_j\\|_j.$$ Every nonzero $J_j^n(X)$ has an occupied block within a fixed number of current blocks of the unit. Its support may extend farther. It has the same central plane-unit copy property as in <a href=\"#obs:smooth-background\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:smooth-background\">[obs:smooth-background]</a>.*\n\n\n\n\n4.  *Choose $H_A$ in <a href=\"#obs:affected-set\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:affected-set\">[obs:affected-set]</a> sufficiently large. For every output block outside $\\mathcal A_j$, in either square, $$\\label{obs:factor-outside}\n     a_{jB}^{\\tau,n}=a_{jB}^{0,n},\\qquad\n                      \\dot a_{jB}^{\\tau,n}=0.$$ For every $B\\in\\mathcal A_j$, the complete sums satisfy $$\\label{obs:factor-copy}\n     a_{jB}^{0,n_0}=a_{jB}^{0,n_1},\\qquad\n     a_{jB}^{\\tau,n_0}=a_{jB}^{\\tau,n_1},\\qquad\n     \\dot a_{jB}^{\\tau,n_0}=\\dot a_{jB}^{\\tau,n_1}.$$ In the fixed small ball, choose $\\rho<1$ with $|a_{jB}^{\\sigma,n}|\\le\\rho$ for $\\sigma\\in\\{0,\\tau\\}$. Then every output block satisfies $$\\label{obs:scalar-log-bound}\n     \\left|\\log\\frac{1+a_{jB}^{\\tau,n}}{1+a_{jB}^{0,n}}\\right|\n           \\le C(U_j+w_j+d_j).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ent-terminal-regulator",
        "exact_quote": "With strict reserve in the parameters of <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> ($1<p_1<p_2$ and $0<h_*<1/p_2$), let $u$ be a later block side compatible with the fixed entry observation grid. Let the square or torus and its $u$-block grid be aligned and compatible, with side $M\\ge2u$. Then, with the terminal covariance $C_{\\ge u}$ from <a href=\"#ent:reference-split\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:reference-split\">[ent:reference-split]</a>, for every compatible padded union $X$ of $u$-blocks and sufficiently small $\\kappa$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[W_u^\\kappa(X,\\zeta)e^{V_u(X,\\zeta)}\\bigr]\n \\le C^{|X|},\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}(\\zeta)=C_{\\ge u}.$$ The terminal Gaussian is on nonconstant modes; the uniform common mean is integrated separately modulo $\\omega$. This bound is uniform as $M/u$ increases.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-terminal-regulator",
          "exact_quote": "With strict reserve in the parameters of <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> ($1<p_1<p_2$ and $0<h_*<1/p_2$), let $u$ be a later block side compatible with the fixed entry observation grid. This bound is uniform as $M/u$ increases.",

      "id": "claim-terminal-regulator-integrability",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-terminal-regulator",
          "exact_quote": "for every compatible padded union $X$ of $u$-blocks and sufficiently small $\\kappa$,",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"

      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-ent-terminal-regulator"
