    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-terminal-family",
        "exact_quote": "Write $$W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)=\\exp\\{\\kappa U_u^{\\rm reg}(X,\\phi)\\}.$$ Here $U_u^{\\rm reg}$ is the sum of gradient energy on $D_u(X)$, the $u$-weighted boundary trace, and the $u^4$-weighted block maxima of squared second differences. Its domain contains all boundary endpoints and fixed difference stencils, with even extension at a free wall. Let $$\\mathfrak R_u^{\\rm b}(X,\\phi)\n       =W_u^{\\kappa_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)e^{V_{u,h_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)}$$ be the regulator used to measure terminal activities, where $V_{u,h}$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> with $h_*=h$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a fixed $1<p_{\\rm seg}<p_1$ with the following property. For a support $X$ of $k$ terminal blocks, put $D=D_{m'}(X)$. Suppose a real deterministic field $h_X$, allowed to depend on $n$ and $X$, is defined on the full domain just described and satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-translation}\n U_{m'}^{\\rm reg}(X,h_X)+\\langle h_X,A_Dh_X\\rangle\\le C_{\\rm tr}k$$ with one uniform constant. Then $$\\label{obs:terminal-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mathfrak R_{m'}^{\\rm b}\n                 (X,\\zeta_{*,n}+h_X)^{p_{\\rm seg}}\\bigr]\\le C^k.$$ The same bound holds at every point of a coupled segment between two such endpoints when their local regulator forms are identical. A random common anchor may be subtracted at each endpoint after neutral projection. The assertion for the segment is a bound on the moment at each parameter value, uniformly in that value.*\n\n\n\n*There are $H_N=O_L(D_N^2)$ terminal blocks in either square. Suppose every unlabelled terminal activity has norm at most $\\nu$ in the weakest norm and put $b_N=CH_N\\nu$. If $b_N=o(1)$, its terminal partition integral $T$ satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-expansion}\n T=1+S_1+O(b_N^2),\\qquad |S_1|\\le b_N,\\qquad\n                  \\log T=S_1+O(b_N^2),$$ where $S_1$ is the sum of single-activity expectations. For a label direction of norm $v$, the derivative obeys $$\\label{obs:terminal-derivative}\n                         |\\dot T|\\le CH_Nv e^{b_N}.$$ These estimates allow the constituent-dependent translations in <a href=\"#obs:terminal-translation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:terminal-translation\">[obs:terminal-translation]</a>; their fixed per-block cost is absorbed by the weight.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-terminal-family",
          "exact_quote": "Write $$W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)=\\exp\\{\\kappa U_u^{\\rm reg}(X,\\phi)\\}.$$ Here $U_u^{\\rm reg}$ is the sum of gradient energy on $D_u(X)$, the $u$-weighted boundary trace, and the $u^4$-weighted block maxima of squared second differences. Its domain contains all boundary endpoints and fixed difference stencils, with even extension at a free wall. Let $$\\mathfrak R_u^{\\rm b}(X,\\phi)\n       =W_u^{\\kappa_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)e^{V_{u,h_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)}$$ be the regulator used to measure terminal activities, where $V_{u,h}$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> with $h_*=h$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a fixed $1<p_{\\rm seg}<p_1$ with the following property. For a support $X$ of $k$ terminal blocks, put $D=D_{m'}(X)$. Suppose a real deterministic field $h_X$, allowed to depend on $n$ and $X$, is defined on the full domain just described and satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-translation}\n U_{m'}^{\\rm reg}(X,h_X)+\\langle h_X,A_Dh_X\\rangle\\le C_{\\rm tr}k$$ with one uniform constant. Then $$\\label{obs:terminal-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mathfrak R_{m'}^{\\rm b}\n                 (X,\\zeta_{*,n}+h_X)^{p_{\\rm seg}}\\bigr]\\le C^k.$$ The same bound holds at every point of a coupled segment between two such endpoints when their local regulator forms are identical. A random common anchor may be subtracted at each endpoint after neutral projection. The assertion for the segment is a bound on the moment at each parameter value, uniformly in that value.*\n\n\n\n*There are $H_N=O_L(D_N^2)$ terminal blocks in either square. Suppose every unlabelled terminal activity has norm at most $\\nu$ in the weakest norm and put $b_N=CH_N\\nu$. If $b_N=o(1)$, its terminal partition integral $T$ satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-expansion}\n T=1+S_1+O(b_N^2),\\qquad |S_1|\\le b_N,\\qquad\n                  \\log T=S_1+O(b_N^2),$$ where $S_1$ is the sum of single-activity expectations. For a label direction of norm $v$, the derivative obeys $$\\label{obs:terminal-derivative}\n                         |\\dot T|\\le CH_Nv e^{b_N}.$$ These estimates allow the constituent-dependent translations in <a href=\"#obs:terminal-translation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:terminal-translation\">[obs:terminal-translation]</a>; their fixed per-block cost is absorbed by the weight.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Write $$W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)=\\exp\\{\\kappa U_u^{\\rm reg}(X,\\phi)\\}.$$ Here $U_u^{\\rm reg}$ is the sum of gradient energy on $D_u(X)$, the $u$-weighted boundary trace, and the $u^4$-weighted block maxima of squared second differences. Its domain contains all boundary endpoints and fixed difference stencils, with even extension at a free wall. Let $$\\mathfrak R_u^{\\rm b}(X,\\phi)\n       =W_u^{\\kappa_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)e^{V_{u,h_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)}$$ be the regulator used to measure terminal activities, where $V_{u,h}$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> with $h_*=h$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a fixed $1<p_{\\rm seg}<p_1$ with the following property. For a support $X$ of $k$ terminal blocks, put $D=D_{m'}(X)$. Suppose a real deterministic field $h_X$, allowed to depend on $n$ and $X$, is defined on the full domain just described and satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-translation}\n U_{m'}^{\\rm reg}(X,h_X)+\\langle h_X,A_Dh_X\\rangle\\le C_{\\rm tr}k$$ with one uniform constant. Then $$\\label{obs:terminal-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mathfrak R_{m'}^{\\rm b}\n                 (X,\\zeta_{*,n}+h_X)^{p_{\\rm seg}}\\bigr]\\le C^k.$$ The same bound holds at every point of a coupled segment between two such endpoints when their local regulator forms are identical. A random common anchor may be subtracted at each endpoint after neutral projection. The assertion for the segment is a bound on the moment at each parameter value, uniformly in that value.*\n\n\n\n*There are $H_N=O_L(D_N^2)$ terminal blocks in either square. Suppose every unlabelled terminal activity has norm at most $\\nu$ in the weakest norm and put $b_N=CH_N\\nu$. If $b_N=o(1)$, its terminal partition integral $T$ satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-expansion}\n T=1+S_1+O(b_N^2),\\qquad |S_1|\\le b_N,\\qquad\n                  \\log T=S_1+O(b_N^2),$$ where $S_1$ is the sum of single-activity expectations. For a label direction of norm $v$, the derivative obeys $$\\label{obs:terminal-derivative}\n                         |\\dot T|\\le CH_Nv e^{b_N}.$$ These estimates allow the constituent-dependent translations in <a href=\"#obs:terminal-translation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:terminal-translation\">[obs:terminal-translation]</a>; their fixed per-block cost is absorbed by the weight.",
      "explanation": "Full regulator and energy control of deterministic translations gives terminal moments and coupled-segment bounds. Small total activity permits a single-activity expansion and a bounded labelled derivative.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {

        },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Free-box critical correlation and threshold** ([source](#eq:critical-definition)): For $r\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$, with $e_1=(1,0)$, set $$\label{eq:critical-definition} \begin{split} C_b(r)&=\lim_{\substack{R\to\infty\\R\ge r}} \mathbb E^{\rm free}_{R,b}\cos(\theta_0-\theta_{re_1}),\\ m(b)&=\lim_{r\to\infty}-\frac1r\log C_b(r),\qquad b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}. \end{split}$$
- **Center magnetization** ([source](#eq:center-definition)): For an integer $n\ge1$, let $$\label{eq:center-definition} M_n=\mathbb E^0_{n,b_c}\cos\theta_0,$$
- **Integral connection** ([source](#eq:bessel-weight)): An *integral connection* is an antisymmetric edge function $\tau:E\to2\pi\mathbb Z$.
- **Bare sector ratio** ([source](#geo:free-sector)): For a graph with the boundary and constant conventions of Section <a href="#sec:heights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:heights">2</a>, write $\Phi_G^\tau=Z_G^\tau/Z_G^0$ for its bare sector ratio.
- **Open observation energy** ([source](#eq:observation-energy)): The corresponding unpinned real Gaussian energy is $$\label{eq:observation-energy} E_G^\tau(y)=\min_u \left\{a^{-1}\lVert \nabla u+\tau\rVert_G^2+\lVert B_su-y\rVert^2\right\}.$$
- **Observation cutoff** ([source](#ent:cutoff-definition)): For the observations of <a href="#eq:observation-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:observation-energy">[eq:observation-energy]</a>, set $$\label{ent:cutoff-definition} \begin{split} w_f&=y_j-y_i+\tau_f,\\ \chi_s(z)&= \frac{\displaystyle\int_{-s}^{s}\exp\{-(z-t)^2/2\}\,\mathrm dt} {\displaystyle\int_{-s}^{s}\exp\{-t^2/2\}\,\mathrm dt},\\ \widehat Z_G^\tau(y)&= Z_G^\tau(y)\prod_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}\chi_s(w_f). \end{split}$$
- **Subset partition functional** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a finite grid $\mathcal B_u$, the subset functional is $$\label{eq:subset-functional} \mathcal Z_u(K;\phi)= \sum_{V\subset\mathcal B_u} \prod_{X\in\mathop{\mathrm{Comp}}(V)}K(X,\phi),$$
- **Observation regulator** ([source](#eq:observation-regulator)): With $D=D_u(X)$, put $$\label{eq:observation-regulator} V_u(X,\phi)=\frac12\sup_{\zeta,e} \left\{h_* E_D^0\bigl(B_s\alpha(\phi+\zeta)+e\bigr) -p_1^{-1}\bigl(\\|\zeta\\|_{C_{<u}}^2+\\|e\\|^2\bigr)\right\},$$
- **Activity norm** ([source](#eq:activity-norm)): The full norm is $$\label{eq:activity-norm} \lVert F\rVert_{u,A_w,V} =\sup_{X,\phi} A_w^{\|X\|}\frac{\|F\|_{u,X,\phi}} {W_u^\kappa(X,\phi)e^{V_u(X,\phi)}}.$$
- **Fixed circulation under Gaussian averaging** ([source](#ent:fixed-circulation)): Indeed, if $y=y^0+B_sv_n$, then $$\label{ent:fixed-circulation} \sigma_z\bigl(\nabla_{\rm c}y^0+\nabla_{\rm c}B_sv_n+\tau\bigr) =\sigma_z(\tau),$$
- **Fully occupied labelled cover** ([source](#ent:occupied-label-cover)): For a component $\pi_\star$ containing the compulsory item, define its occupied support to be exactly $$\label{ent:occupied-label-cover} X(\pi_\star)=\mathfrak C_{\star,m} \ \cup\! \bigcup_{\substack{I\in\pi_\star\\I\ne I_\star}} \operatorname{cov}_m(I).$$
- **Normal trajectory coordinates** ([source](#trj:normal-coordinates)): Define, for this history, $$\label{trj:normal-coordinates} u_j=(T_j^g)^{-1}(T_j),\qquad Q_j=\mathcal R_j-\mathcal R_j^g(u_j),\qquad Q_j=Q_{e,j}+Q_{o,j},$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-height-coefficient | a(b_c) | = | $8\pi$ | The critical parameter defined by the free-box correlation mass |
| q-critical-correlation-equivalent | C_{b_c}(r) | = | $B_{\rm XY}\,r^{-1/4}(\log r)^{1/8}(1+o(1))$ | Critical inverse temperature; axial integer separation tending to infinity |
| q-shape-amplitude | c_{\rm shape} | = | $\exp\left\{\frac{4\pi^2}{a}R_D(0,0)\right\}$ | The bulk-to-center comparison with the prescribed thermodynamic limit |
| q-entry-ordinary-smallness | \\|K_{\mathcal G,m}^\tau\\|_{m,A_w,V} | ≤ | $Ce^{-cR}$ | Fixed sufficiently large isolation scale followed by sufficiently large admissible cell side |
| q-entry-label-growth | \\|J_{\mathcal G,m}\\|_{m,A_w,V} | ≤ | $C_Rs^{C_R}$ | Fixed preceding parameters; admissible cell side and finite square volume |
| q-gradient-history-decay | t_j^{(k)} | = | $\frac{1}{2(\log L)j} +O\!\left(\frac{\log(2+j)}{j^2}\right),$ | Fixed interior windows at absolute scale index |
| q-fundamental-history-decay | z_j^{(k)} | = | $O(j^{-1})$ | Fixed interior history windows |
| q-history-remainder-decay | \\|\mathcal R_j^{(k)}\\|_{j,k} | = | $O(j^{-2})$ | Fixed interior windows; norms from different histories are not subtracted |
| q-parity-limit-coefficient | b_* | = | $2\log L$ | Fixed nearest-neighbor critical reference and block ratio |
| q-quadratic-coefficient-convergence | \|H_j-H\|+\|b_j-b_*\| | ≤ | $C(1+j)^{-2}$ | Sufficiently large absolute index |
| q-reference-circulation-energy | g_{L^N} | = | $2\pi(\log L)N+c_G+o(1)$ | Geometric square sizes tending to infinity |
| q-consecutive-comparison-error | Consecutive center logarithmic comparison error | = | $O\left(\frac{D_N^4}{N^2}+\frac{1}{ND_N}\right)$ | The growing terminal block ratio specified in the consecutive-size proposition |
| q-center-magnetization-equivalent | M_{L^N} | = | $b_M(L^N)^{-1/8}\bigl(\log L^N\bigr)^{1/16}(1+o(1)).$ | Geometric sizes; the geometric comparison subsequently extends the equivalent to all integer sizes |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ent-initialization",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the finite list of map norms and reserves just described. There are constants $C,c>0$ such that the following holds for every sufficiently large fixed dyadic $R$, with $P=R^5$ and the fixed period $q$. For every sufficiently large dyadic $s$ satisfying <a href=\"#ent:entry-side\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:entry-side\">[ent:entry-side]</a>, the assertions below hold uniformly in the finite volume. The threshold on $s$ may depend on all preceding choices. A compatible torus or aligned free square has side at least $sP$; for a marked square its side is larger than the fixed multiple of $sP$ needed to contain the compulsory region and its collars. In the square applications its ratio to $sP$ tends to infinity.*\n\n1.  *There are plane activities $K_m^0$ which are real on real fields, negation-even, invariant under common translation by $\\omega$, and covariant under block translations and square symmetries. There are centered torus and free-square activities $K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0$ with $$\\|K_m^0\\|_{m,A_w,V},\n                      \\ \\|K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0\\|_{m,A_w,V}\n                             \\le Ce^{-cR}$$ in each of the fixed norms. On every such finite graph a positive field-independent scalar $c_{\\mathcal G,m}$ makes <a href=\"#ent:ordinary-subset\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:ordinary-subset\">[ent:ordinary-subset]</a> hold. If $D_m(X)$ unwraps in a torus, its activity is exactly the plane activity. The same is true in a free square when $D_m(X)$ misses the wall.*\n\n\n2.  *In a free square with the center unit there are real periodic activities $K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau$ and $J_{\\mathcal G,m}$ for which <a href=\"#ent:labelled-subset\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:labelled-subset\">[ent:labelled-subset]</a> holds with that same scalar. For a constant $C_R<\\infty$ independent of $s,n$, $$\\label{ent:label-norm}\n     \\|K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau\\|_{m,A_w,V}\\le Ce^{-cR},\n     \\qquad\n     \\|J_{\\mathcal G,m}\\|_{m,A_w,V}\\le C_Rs^{C_R}.$$ The sector norm may use the slightly weaker weight and stronger regulator already reserved above.*\n\n    *There is a fixed set $\\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}$ of at most $C_R$ entry blocks, containing the full inflation of a square of side a fixed large multiple of $P$ in observation-cell units around the source, such that $$\\label{ent:compulsory-support}\n           J_{\\mathcal G,m}(X)=0\n                      \\quad\\hbox{unless}\\quad\n                      \\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}\\subset X.$$ Every block of $\\mathfrak C_{\\star,m}$ is occupied in $X$ in the subset functional. The full support of each summand of $J(X)$ also occupies every padded dependency and the complete connecting, winding, or wall-reaching tag of every item in that summand.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If the rectangle spanned by $D_m(X)$ misses the source, then $$\\label{ent:translated-copy}\n     K_{\\mathcal G,m}^\\tau(X,\\psi)\n                 =K_{\\mathcal G,m}^0(X,\\psi+\\lambda_X),$$ where $\\lambda_X$ is its local lift. This assertion uses the finite-square centered prescription if the support is near a wall. There are also centered-unit plane prescriptions $K_{\\infty,m}^\\tau$ and $J_{\\infty,m}$. Whenever $D_m(X)$ misses the square wall under the centered aligned identification just specified, its prescription is exactly the plane one with background translated by $$\\label{ent:plane-background}\n                             (v_n-v_\\infty)/\\alpha .$$ This identity holds for both the unlabelled and labelled activities. The sufficient regions in all three copy statements include the padded supports and all occupied tags. The plane centered-unit prescriptions obey the same bounds as in <a href=\"#ent:label-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ent:label-norm\">[ent:label-norm]</a>, with their local plane norms.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-trj-parity-interface",
          "exact_quote": "There are a fixed small tube, $\\vartheta<1$, and $C<\\infty$, uniform over the entry sides above, on which the exact map satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n u_+-u={}&-H_jZ^2+\n O\\bigl((|u|+Z^2)(Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)+\\|Q_e\\|^2\n                    +\\|Q_o\\|(|Z|+\\|Q_o\\|)\\bigr),\n                                      \\label{trj:u-map}\\\\\n Z_+-Z={}&-b_juZ+\n O\\bigl((u^2+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)|Z|\n       +( |u|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|+\\|Q_o\\|^2)\\|Q_o\\|\\bigr),\n                                      \\label{trj:z-map}\\\\\n \\|Q_{e,+}\\|\\le{}&\\vartheta\\|Q_e\\|\n                         +C(|Z|+\\|Q_o\\|)^2,\n                                      \\label{trj:even-map}\\\\\n \\|Q_{o,+}\\|\\le{}&\\vartheta\\|Q_o\\|\n                         +C(|u|+Z^2+\\|Q_e\\|)|Z|.\n                                      \\label{trj:odd-map}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The error terms have the differentiated Taylor bounds of the displayed orders. The coefficients, independent of the entry side at a common absolute $j$, obey fixed positive upper and lower bounds for sufficiently large $j$. Their limits are $H>0$ and $$\\label{trj:b-value}\n                       b_* =\\frac{a}{4\\pi}\\log L=2\\log L.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-consecutive",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\epsilon<1/10$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $n_0=L^N$, $n_1=L^{N+1}$, and choose a power $m'=L^{j'}$ so that $$\\label{obs:stop}\n D_N=\\frac{n_0}{m'}\\asymp N^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n j'=N-O(\\log N).$$ Use the entry history for $N$ in both squares, and let $t_{j'}$ be its unbalanced plane gradient coefficient at the stop. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-duality): Fourier expansion, divergence constraints, and rotation of currents onto dual gradients. -> Positive Bessel-height partition ratios for the bulk and center observables.
- **step-affine-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-comparisons): Fixed-graph lattice-Gaussian subdivision limit and release to independent primary increments. -> Precision monotonicity and primary-reference-cost moment estimates.
- **step-critical-height-inputs** [claim](#stmt-prop-height-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-height-inputs): Use the companion critical coefficient and fixed-geometry mixed height limits at their stated scope. -> Gaussian height limits and sampled finite-observation guard approximations.
- **step-pinned-trial** [claim](#stmt-geo-pinned-sector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-pinned-sector): Confine cut components, evaluate single-valued lifts, and restore the strip with vanishing averaged losses while retaining the rim pin. -> Pinned-rim lower bound by the smooth trial energy.
- **step-geometric-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-geo-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-pinned-sector-2): Attach identical annular nests, cancel their microscopic ratios, and bracket the thermodynamic correlation by pinned and free comparators. -> Center regular variation, rough center decay, and the positive bulk-to-center shape factor.
- **step-local-map-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-map): Group inputs by coarse closure, relocate designated small linear pieces, and retain scalar inverse decorations in the normalized map. -> Exact partition map and analytic contraction estimates.
- **step-unit-background** [claim](#stmt-ent-center-background) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-center-background): Rotate differentiated primal Green fields and compare the square and plane finite-range tails. -> Bounded branches, spatial difference bounds, and source-free lifts.
- **step-entry-regulator** [claim](#stmt-ent-entry-regulator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-entry-regulator): Restrict trial fields to open components and use even reflection within the range buffer. -> Compatible accumulated and shell regulator estimates.
- **step-cutoff-bounds** [claim](#stmt-ent-cutoff-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-cutoff-bounds): Transport cell-average differences by bounded-cost flows, apply tilted affine comparison, and combine Gaussian cutoff tails with a face union bound. -> Prescribed-observation suppression and negligible physical cutoff loss.
- **step-face-localization** [claim](#stmt-ent-face-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-face-localization): Construct local face trials, separate the angular junction energy, and prove exponential inverse-energy locality by cell coercivity. -> Localized face matrices and the guarded deletion-versus-patch estimate.
- **step-analytic-pattern-bound** [claim](#stmt-ent-pattern-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-pattern-estimate): Normalize affected halos, retain tilted guard-law factors, classify actual box energies, complete localized Gaussian costs, and interpolate analytic strips. -> Full derivative-norm pattern gains with only polynomial compulsory-source cost.
- **step-entry-construction** [claim](#stmt-ent-initialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-pattern-estimate-2): Sum connected full-cover patterns by rooted trees and control translated entries using the reserved weights and regulators. -> Exact ordinary and labelled subset identities, entry norms, and sufficient copy conditions.
- **step-parity-interface** [claim](#stmt-trj-parity-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-parity-interface): Balance triangular linear rows, remove the exact Gaussian curve, and use half-period parity in the analytic Taylor map. -> Normal scalar recursion and contracting parity remainders.
- **step-coefficient-rate** [claim](#stmt-trj-coefficient-rate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-coefficient-rate): Truncate future balancing rows, estimate finite-depth Gaussian jets and rectangular sums, and balance the depth error against geometric tails. -> Absolute-index rate for both quadratic coefficients.
- **step-physical-torus** [claim](#stmt-trj-physical-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-physical-torus): Bracket smooth-shift covariance by free cuts and pinned rims, prove Laplace limits, then use a fixed-graph replica identity for mean-character factorization. -> Gaussian mean-free diagnostics and factorization, including compatible observation versions.
- **step-confinement-and-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-trj-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-vanishing): Read both scalar directions on independently initialized stopping tori, exclude first exits, and apply remainder contraction to interior limsups. -> Uniform smallness and interior vanishing for plane and torus histories.
- **step-odd-cone** [claim](#stmt-trj-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-cone): Combine the physical mean-character lower bound with odd-remainder convolution to force cone entry, then convolve parity recursions. -> Nonzero slowly varying odd signal and sharpened transverse bounds.
- **step-history-overlap** [claim](#stmt-trj-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trj-overlap): Compare each stopped torus to its moving Gaussian curve and cancel the same unknown finite physical variance and phase signal between histories. -> Overlap bounds for normal gradient and odd magnitude.
- **step-critical-trajectory** [claim](#stmt-trj-trajectory) / [proof](#pm-h-32): Splice histories, absorb sparse switching errors by sampled barriers, estimate the separatrix defect, and sum reciprocal increments. -> Precise unbalanced gradient decay and the fundamental and remainder bounds on every useful window.
- **step-paired-local-maps** [claim](#stmt-obs-local-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-local-estimates): Designate centered constants before inserting local backgrounds, keep full obstruction supports, and derive wall, defect, and label contractions. -> Exact complete-scalar copying and cancellation with contracting local errors.
- **step-terminal-regulator** [claim](#stmt-ent-terminal-regulator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-terminal-regulator): Bound positive-order Gaussian trace and combine old and terminal open-energy contraction in Gaussian determinants. -> Terminal regulator moments uniform over growing block grids.
- **step-terminal-families** [claim](#stmt-obs-terminal-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-terminal-family): Compare translated regulators to stronger reserved regulators, use convex coupled-segment moments, and sum compatible families by rooted occupied size. -> Single-activity terminal expansion and labelled derivative bound.
- **step-consecutive-center-comparison** [claim](#stmt-obs-consecutive) / [proof](#pm-h-37): Evaluate the circulation Green energy, cancel paired extracted scalars, discard the surviving label relatively, and account for every terminal support class. -> Consecutive center logarithmic ratio with summable error and convergent Green constant.
- **step-amplitude-and-bulk-equivalent** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-terminal-family-2): Telescope unweighted Green differences, sum weighted differences by parts, extract the finite center amplitude, and transfer the equivalent through geometric comparisons. -> Positive finite critical bulk amplitude with the claimed logarithmic correction.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-critical-correlation-equivalent**: The critical axial free-box thermodynamic correlation has a power-law decay with a multiplicative logarithmic correction and a positive finite amplitude.
- **invariant-boundary-sensitive-duality**: Spin correlations and center magnetization are positive dual sector partition ratios, with different height boundary and circulation conventions for the two observables.
- **invariant-complete-compulsory-support**: The labelled entry component occupies the full compulsory source region and every attached padded dependency and obstruction tag.
- **invariant-absolute-interior-trajectory**: The gradient singleton decays harmonically with a summable correction on controlled interior ranges of the size-dependent histories; the scale index remains absolute.
- **invariant-complete-scalar-copy**: The complete centered, unit-sector, and labelled extraction coefficients agree between the paired squares in the affected central region, enabling exact cancellation of their extracted factors.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-off-critical-fit**: Apply the critical correlation equivalent at another inverse temperature. -> The stated equivalent supplies no correlation law away from the critical threshold.
- **anti-use-finite-volume-certificate**: Treat agreement in finite boxes or at microscopic separations as a certificate of the thermodynamic equivalent. -> Finite observations do not establish the ordered limits or control the asymptotic remainder.
- **anti-use-altered-spin-model**: Transfer the equivalent directly to a different interaction, lattice, or boundary convention. -> The model-specific Fourier weights and geometric comparisons no longer follow from the stated assumptions.
- **anti-use-uniform-microscopic-guard-limit**: Replace conditional guard estimates by convergence uniform at every microscopic guard value. -> The conditional input only provides sampled finite-observation approximations with an ordered enlargement of the observation list.
- **anti-use-label-support-erasure**: Compress a labelled activity to a root block while dropping its padded dependencies or winding and wall-reaching tags. -> The reduced support loses exclusion and ambient-obstruction information required for exact copying and scalar cancellation.
- **anti-use-reset-scale-or-global-trajectory**: Reset the running scale at entry or use the trajectory estimate outside its controlled history windows. -> The harmonic coefficient and its error are established at absolute indices on fixed interior ranges; a uniformly valid microscopic start is not supplied.
- **anti-use-quantitative-amplitude-estimator**: Read an explicit amplitude value or a certified finite-distance error tolerance from the leading equivalent. -> Existence of the positive finite amplitude and a relative asymptotic remainder do not provide an effective evaluation procedure or a finite-distance accuracy threshold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result is an asymptotic equivalent for an infinite-volume correlation, obtained through ordered thermodynamic, geometric, and multiscale limits. The source explicitly states: "The thermodynamic limit in the first line is always taken before the separation tends to infinity." Its amplitude argument also states: "The preceding comparison gives a convergent amplitude after the leading power and logarithm have been removed." Neither statement supplies a finite decision procedure, an effective amplitude evaluation, or a finite-distance remainder certificate. Finite lattice calculations could check auxiliary identities but would not decide this asymptotic result.

No bounded-variation assertion about $(c_N)$ is needed.",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The correlation equivalent supplies an asymptotic mathematical specification, but no finite error threshold or executable correlation evaluator suitable for a software acceptance contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The specified cosine spin measure can inform physical simulation targets, but the paper does not construct a sampling engine or establish its finite-volume accuracy. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The physical torus diagnostics provide theoretical calibration observables, but their convergence has no supplied quantitative threshold from which to build a certified diagnostic tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exact local map operates on analytic activity functions and Gaussian integrations. Its existence and contraction estimates do not supply a finite representation, truncation algorithm, or certified evaluation procedure for those functions. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Occupied covers record dependencies, winding paths, and wall obstructions within the proof. The paper gives no storage interface or finite representation of the associated activity functions. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coefficient analysis bounds symbolic propagation and Gaussian kernel errors, but provides no accelerator implementation, execution schedule, or hardware cost contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Integral gauge transformations preserve height partition ratios by reindexing. This symmetry supplies no cryptographic construction, adversarial model, or security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional Gaussian prediction estimates control harmonic extensions in the proof; they do not define a learned predictor, training procedure, or generalisation contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite observation energy is a concrete quadratic minimisation problem. On the specified cell graphs, the proof constructs a coercive correction operator and evaluates the minimum by solving its linear system. This supports adapting the finite Gaussian proxy as a constrained energy calculation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rooted tree of cut blocks organises exact partition summation and component constants. It does not provide a distributed protocol or guarantees under communication failures. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal contribution belongs to mathematical physics: it establishes a critical correlation equivalent and a positive microscopic amplitude. It does not yield a finite engineering evaluator for that amplitude. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-model-and-critical-parameter** (hypothesis): The critical equivalent concerns the nearest-neighbor cosine interaction on the square lattice at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature. It does not establish the same equivalent for another interaction, lattice, or temperature. Blocked use: ['Transferring the logarithmic correction to an altered spin model without establishing its critical inputs.', 'Applying the critical equivalent away from the specified critical parameter.']
- **limit-ordered-thermodynamic-separation** (hypothesis): The free-box thermodynamic limit must precede the limit of large axial separation. Blocked use: ['Replacing the ordered limits by a simultaneous growth of box size and separation.', 'Using a bounded-box correlation as the thermodynamic observable.']
- **limit-asymptotic-error-and-amplitude** (nonconstructive): The leading equivalent establishes a positive finite microscopic amplitude and a vanishing relative error, but supplies no numerical amplitude value or effective finite-separation accuracy threshold. Blocked use: ['Using the equivalent as a certified finite-distance acceptance tolerance.', 'Assigning the microscopic amplitude from the universal exponents alone.']
- **limit-dual-boundary-conventions** (hypothesis): The bulk correlation and center magnetization use different dual boundary and circulation conventions. Exterior copies may be identified only while retaining every edge. Blocked use: ['Replacing the pinned exterior of the free spin box by an unpinned height constant.', 'Dropping incident edges when identifying exterior copies.', 'Treating the center-unit sector as the two-source bulk sector.']
- **limit-affine-comparison-tilt** (edge_case): A fixed real linear tilt preserves the stated precision monotonicity, but its shifted-to-centered ratio need not be bounded above by one. Blocked use: ['Applying the untilted upper bound to a tilted partition ratio.']
- **limit-released-component-constants** (hypothesis): Released constants must be quotiented or confined under the stated convention, and a real statistic used when releasing a constant must annihilate it. Blocked use: ['Estimating a nonconstant-invariant statistic after releasing an unconfined component constant.', 'Summing an unconstrained translation mode without the prescribed quotient.']
- **limit-fixed-positive-geometry** (hypothesis): The mixed height limits require the specified fixed polygonal geometries, positive-width passages, and permitted positive-area pins. The sector limits used in the geometric comparison retain holes of positive radius. Blocked use: ['Applying the mixed limit to components joined only at a point.', 'Letting a hole collapse to a microscopic source inside the same fixed-geometry mesh limit.', 'Using an unpermitted degenerate pin geometry.']
- **limit-sampled-guard-approximations** (approximation): Conditional height limits provide sampled finite-observation approximations. The observation list is enlarged after the lattice limit; uniform convergence at every microscopic guard value is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Replacing sampled guard-law estimates by a uniform pointwise bound over all microscopic guard data.', 'Increasing the observation list during the lattice limit without additional justification.']
- **limit-restoration-factor-convergence** (approximation): Event transfer and bounds on conditional restoration factors do not supply an additional convergence theorem for the restoration factor itself. Blocked use: ['Inferring convergence of a restoration factor from the sampled pin and conditional Laplace limits.']
- **limit-pin-preserving-gauge** (hypothesis): The pinned-rim trial bound requires the branch to vanish near the pin and permits only gauges whose potentials vanish on every pin component. Blocked use: ['Changing the connection by a gauge that changes retained zero-pin data.', 'Using a trial branch that is nonzero near the pinned rim.']
- **limit-analytic-map-input-and-buffer** (hypothesis): The local map requires periodic local activities, finite point bounds, a sufficiently small unlabelled activity ball, and the specified shell range buffer on compatible finite grids. Blocked use: ['Applying shell estimates to nonlocal field dependence or incompatible grids.', 'Running the shell too near the finite-volume scale.', 'Interpreting analytic activity existence as a finite executable evaluator.']
- **limit-large-support-relocation** (hypothesis): Only pieces from small linear inputs may be assigned to singletons. Large residuals retain their whole coarse closure, including occupied obstruction information. Blocked use: ['Localizing a large or nonlinear residual solely because its numerical value happens to be constant.', 'Erasing a wall or winding tag before testing support smallness.']
- **limit-cutoff-test-class** (hypothesis): The physical cutoff-loss estimate applies to the listed bounded centered tests and controlled centered polynomial-Laplace tests. In the square unit sector it is stated for the partition integral, with centered normalization. Blocked use: ['Extending the unit-sector cutoff estimate to arbitrary unbounded insertion observables.', 'Treating an absolute centered-normalized cutoff loss as a relative insertion error without a lower bound for that insertion.']
- **limit-free-face-energy-family** (hypothesis): Face-energy localization concerns the specified free-energy graph family with whole observation rows and inherited cuts. Temporary hard primary guards are excluded, and the residual face matrix need not be positive. Blocked use: ['Using the face comparison theorem on graphs with temporary hard guards.', 'Replacing a patch by a topology that omits inherited cuts or retained observations.', 'Treating the residual face matrix as a positive quadratic penalty.']
- **limit-entry-threshold-and-temperature** (nonconstructive): Entry parameters and reserves are fixed before the admissible observation-cell side is taken sufficiently large. The threshold may depend on preceding choices, and the initialization makes no uniform temperature claim. Blocked use: ['Choosing an effective entry threshold from the qualitative convergence argument alone.', 'Using one entry bound uniformly over temperature.', 'Reversing the prescribed parameter order.']
- **limit-complete-labelled-occupancy** (hypothesis): The compulsory labelled support includes the entire compulsory cover and every attached padded dependency, connecting path, winding tag, and wall-reaching tag. Blocked use: ['Compressing the labelled support to its root block.', 'Testing exact copying after removing occupied obstruction tags.', 'Treating an auxiliary comparison container as an activity input.']
- **limit-pattern-order-and-correlated-guards** (approximation): The conditional discrepancy estimate is obtained after fixing the geometry, threshold, observation compact set, and finite moment orders. It uses correlated guard laws and gives no rate in the observation-cell side or convergence uniform in all orders. Blocked use: ['Treating distinct guards as independent.', 'Letting the required moment order grow without repeating the fixed-target approximation argument.', 'Extracting an observation-cell convergence rate from the conditional discrepancy bound.']
- **limit-absolute-history-windows** (hypothesis): Trajectory estimates use an absolute scale index on fixed interior windows of size-dependent histories. They do not provide a single uniformly valid microscopic start. Blocked use: ['Resetting the scale index at entry.', 'Applying the harmonic trajectory estimate outside its stated interior windows.', 'Subtracting remainder norms belonging to different entry histories.']
- **limit-distinct-torus-remainders** (hypothesis): Physical stopping tori are initialized separately for their target sizes. Their singleton coefficients copy the plane history, but their remainders are not identified with the plane remainder. Blocked use: ['Continuing a stopping torus from a different size.', 'Replacing a torus remainder by the plane remainder in the physical diagnostic argument.']
- **limit-coefficient-rate-versus-height-rate** (approximation): The quadratic coefficient rate comes from explicit Gaussian kernels and finite-order local algebra. It does not establish a quantitative convergence rate for the physical height scaling limit. Blocked use: ['Using the coefficient convergence estimate as a finite-size height-limit error bound.']
- **limit-complete-scalar-cancellation** (hypothesis): Paired cancellation requires equality of the complete centered scalar, complete unit scalar, and complete labelled derivative separately. Equality of their differences alone does not identify their ratios. Blocked use: ['Cancelling extracted factors after checking only the difference between unit and centered scalars.', 'Discarding remote scalar decorations without tracking their occupied support.']
- **limit-terminal-translation-and-smallness** (hypothesis): Terminal translations require control of the full ordinary regulator and internal energy on the padded domain. Coupled-segment moments require identical local forms, and the terminal expansion requires the total activity parameter to vanish. Blocked use: ['Replacing full translation control by an internal energy estimate alone.', 'Assuming a moment bound for a supremum over the coupled segment.', 'Using the single-activity expansion when the total terminal activity is not small.']
- **limit-green-remainder-summation** (edge_case): The Green-energy remainder converges, and weighted differences are handled by summation by parts. The proof does not require or establish bounded variation of that remainder. Blocked use: ['Assuming absolute summability of the unweighted Green-remainder increments from convergence alone.']
- **limit-no-finite-certificate-for-main-equivalent** (formal_scope): Finite checks of auxiliary lattice or Gaussian identities do not decide the infinite-volume critical equivalent or certify its microscopic amplitude. Blocked use: ['Reporting finite auxiliary calculations as a certificate of the asymptotic theorem.']

          "anchor": "stmt-geo-pinned-sector",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a bounded connected polygonal Lipschitz domain in the fixed-geometry class of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:height-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:height-inputs\">4</a>. Let $\\mathsf F$ be an aligned union of positive-area boxes with dyadic vertices in $U$, permitted as a pin in that fixed geometry. Suppose a simple dyadic rectilinear arc joins interiors of sides of the two holes transversely and has a neighborhood at positive distance from $\\mathsf F$ and all nonincident boundaries. Give the holes periods $+2\\pi,-2\\pi$. Let $\\vartheta=dv$ be a smooth closed one-form near $\\overline U$ with these periods, with a multivalued primitive whose only jump is across that arc and whose branch is zero on a neighborhood of $\\mathsf F$. Choose the canonical integral connections obtained from the jumps of this branch along a coherent rounded discretization of the arc, or connections related to them by integral gauges whose potentials vanish on every component of $\\mathsf F$.         }

          "exact_quote": "Assume every activity has period $\\omega$ under common field translations, and its field dependence lies in the sites of its occupied support $X$ together with a fixed microscopic collar of width at most a fixed small fraction of $u$. The norm padding $D_u(X)$ is generally larger. Call an input small when its connected support has $|X|\\le n_{\\mathrm{sm}}$, for the fixed block threshold of the calculus. For a linear direction assume the point bound $$|F|_{u,X,\\phi}\\le\n \\nu_X W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)e^{V_u(X,\\phi)}\n \\qquad\\text{for every real }\\phi,$$ with $\\nu_X<\\infty$; the resulting estimates are homogeneous in $\\nu_X$.*\n\n*For a sufficiently large fixed dyadic $L$, sufficiently large weights $A_w$, and a sufficiently small activity ball, shell integration and designated localizations on small linear inputs give the exact identity $$\\label{eq:exact-local-map}\n \\mathbb E_\\zeta\\mathcal Z_u(K;\\phi+\\zeta)\n =\\prod_{B\\in\\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_B)\\,\n        \\mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\\phi).$$ The map is analytic in the activities. If the total input size, including separately measured singleton coefficients, is $\\nu$, its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,A_w}\\nu^2$, with the corresponding differentiated bounds. A residual from a large input retains its whole coarse closure; only a piece from a small linear input can be assigned to a singleton. The coefficient $a_B$ is the complete sum of the field-independent pieces designated at $B$. Any undesignated constant value of a translated nonconstant piece remains in $K'$.*\n\n*Write $\\Gamma_u=C_{<Lu}-C_{<u}$ for the shell from $u$ to $Lu$. For its estimates on the aligned block and observation grids of a finite square or torus of side $M$, assume $Lu\\le M/D_0$, with $D_0$ a sufficiently large fixed range buffer. The norms <a href=\"#eq:point-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:point-norm\">[eq:point-norm]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:activity-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:activity-norm\">[eq:activity-norm]</a> have the shell composition, localization, charge, and large-support contractions of [[B](#ref-B), Sections 4 and 8, Lemma 9.2]. In particular, the extra regulator is monotone under enlargement, adds on compatible separated supports, and costs $C^{|X|}$ under shell averaging. On the full bulk block grid, a small neutral input invariant under field negation has gain $CL^{-3}$ after constant and quadratic localization. Removing only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for a neutral input invariant under field negation and $CL^{-1}$ for a general neutral input, including at irregular end blocks. For a charge-$k$ component of a small input, $k\\ne0$, the Gaussian charge gain is $$\\exp\\left\\{\n -\\bigl(\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}|k|\\alpha-\\tfrac12\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}^2\\bigr)\n       \\Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)+h_0|k|\\alpha\\right\\}.$$ Here a charge-$k$ component obeys $F(\\phi+c)=e^{ik\\alpha c}F(\\phi)$, $v_0$ is a reference site in a block of $X$, and $\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}>0$ is the Cameron-shift amplitude. The analytic radius must satisfy $h_0>C_{\\mathrm{sh}}\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}$, where $C_{\\mathrm{sh}}$ is the fixed geometric constant for that shift, and the parameters are chosen so that $\\beta_{\\mathrm{sh}}\\Gamma_u(v_0,v_0)>h_0$ by a uniform fixed margin.           "anchor": "stmt-obs-terminal-family",
          "exact_quote": "Write $$W_u^\\kappa(X,\\phi)=\\exp\\{\\kappa U_u^{\\rm reg}(X,\\phi)\\}.$$ Here $U_u^{\\rm reg}$ is the sum of gradient energy on $D_u(X)$, the $u$-weighted boundary trace, and the $u^4$-weighted block maxima of squared second differences. Its domain contains all boundary endpoints and fixed difference stencils, with even extension at a free wall. Let $$\\mathfrak R_u^{\\rm b}(X,\\phi)\n       =W_u^{\\kappa_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)e^{V_{u,h_{\\rm b}}(X,\\phi)}$$ be the regulator used to measure terminal activities, where $V_{u,h}$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:observation-regulator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:observation-regulator\">[eq:observation-regulator]</a> with $h_*=h$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a fixed $1<p_{\\rm seg}<p_1$ with the following property. For a support $X$ of $k$ terminal blocks, put $D=D_{m'}(X)$. Suppose a real deterministic field $h_X$, allowed to depend on $n$ and $X$, is defined on the full domain just described and satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-translation}\n U_{m'}^{\\rm reg}(X,h_X)+\\langle h_X,A_Dh_X\\rangle\\le C_{\\rm tr}k$$ with one uniform constant. Then $$\\label{obs:terminal-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mathfrak R_{m'}^{\\rm b}\n                 (X,\\zeta_{*,n}+h_X)^{p_{\\rm seg}}\\bigr]\\le C^k.$$ The same bound holds at every point of a coupled segment between two such endpoints when their local regulator forms are identical. A random common anchor may be subtracted at each endpoint after neutral projection. The assertion for the segment is a bound on the moment at each parameter value, uniformly in that value.*\n\n\n\n*There are $H_N=O_L(D_N^2)$ terminal blocks in either square. Suppose every unlabelled terminal activity has norm at most $\\nu$ in the weakest norm and put $b_N=CH_N\\nu$. If $b_N=o(1)$, its terminal partition integral $T$ satisfies $$\\label{obs:terminal-expansion}\n T=1+S_1+O(b_N^2),\\qquad |S_1|\\le b_N,\\qquad\n                  \\log T=S_1+O(b_N^2),$$ where $S_1$ is the sum of single-activity expectations. For a label direction of norm $v$, the derivative obeys $$\\label{obs:terminal-derivative}\n                         |\\dot T|\\le CH_Nv e^{b_N}.$$ These estimates allow the constituent-dependent translations in <a href=\"#obs:terminal-translation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:terminal-translation\">[obs:terminal-translation]</a>; their fixed per-block cost is absorbed by the weight.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-obs-terminal-family",
          "exact_quote": "The internal energy alone would not control the ordinary regulator: its second-difference term also appears in",

        },

        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the critical logarithmic correction for the nearest-neighbor cosine XY model on the square lattice. Let $`C_{b_c}(r)`$ be the two-point correlation at critical inverse temperature, obtained by taking the free-box thermodynamic limit while the two sites remain <i>r</i> lattice steps apart along a coordinate axis. Then

$`\displaystyle C_{b_c}(r)=B_{\mathrm{XY}}r^{-1/4}(\log r)^{1/8}(1+o(1)), \qquad B_{\mathrm{XY}}\in(0,\infty),`$

as $`r\to\infty`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-prop-local-map](#stmt-prop-local-map) | proposition | `prop:local-map` | 7 | Assume every activity has period $\omega$ under common field translations, and its field dependence lies in the sites of its occupied support $X$ together with a fixed microscopic collar of width at m |
